# Introduction


## Owner Manual

Introduction
Congratulations and thank you for choosing the VF 8.
This Owner’s Manual describes all the features and operations of your new VinFast vehicle and contains important maintenance and safety related information designed to keep you safe. Information will be updated continuously throughout the customer’s vehicle usage.
The information contained in this manual is specific to your vehicle, but certain features, functions, and display instructions may not be applicable, depending on your market region or the software version of the vehicle.
The content in the manual has the latest update status as of the vehicle’s launch, but due to the optimization and addition of vehicle functions, some interface diagrams or vehicle images may differ from the actual vehicle.
Thoroughly familiarize yourself with the contents of this Owner’s Manual prior to operation of your vehicle.
Owner Manual Version: VF8_MY26_VN_ENG_1.0.1
Owner Manual Information
This Owner Manual will familiarize you with all the features, functions, equipment, and maintenance needs of your vehicle.
An initial overview of the vehicle is covered in the next section and provides relevant operating information, including the name and location of the key exterior and interior features and components.
Warning, Caution and Notes
The Owner’s Manual contains safety precautions and operating procedures. Failure to follow these safety warnings and instructions may lead to serious injury or even death.
To highlight content of importance, the following message formats will be shown:
WARNING
Critical information, often involving safety related content that if not followed could result in death or serious injury.
CAUTION
Important information relating to a feature, function, or situation,which if ignored, could result in damage to your vehicle.
NOTE
Key points of useful information.
It is recommended that you have your vehicle inspected by an authorized service center for all intermediate and major repairs. It is essential that the vehicle is repaired in accordance with VinFast specifications. Owners may perform minor self fix tasks if suggested by this Owner’s Manual or highlighted in vehicle alerts page. For all self-fix tasks, clear instructions will be provided. If the instructions provided are unclear, please contact VinFast service before proceeding.
Using the Quick Reference guide
A printed Quick reference guide is provided with your vehicle and is placed in the glove box at the time of vehicle delivery.
The fastest way to find information on a particular topic is by using the Table of Contents.
Using the Digital Manual
An online interactive version of the owner manual can be accessed on any compatible Web-browser, as well as on the VinFast App. An integrated version of this owner manual is also available to view in vehicle on the Infotainment screen.
To access the Integrated Owner’s Manual in your vehicle, follow the steps below:
Tap “App Library” on the Infotainment screen.
Tap “Owner’s Manual”.
Use Touchscreen and search functionality to locate required content.
NOTE
To update the latest Owner’s Manual, please connect to the Internet, open the Owner’s Manual app, and wait about 5-10 minutes.
Applicable Content & Images
The information and specifications contained in this Owner’s manual are current at the time of publication. However, as part of VinFast’s policy of continuous product improvement, specifications, features, and other information may be changed at any time without prior notice.
This Owner’s manual is intended to provide general guidance for all variants of the VF 8 model. Some features and functions described in this guide may not apply to your vehicle configuration or may not be available or activated at the time your vehicle is delivered. This includes, but is not limited to, applications integrated into the infotainment system and the VinFast app.
The illustrations and images provided in this manual are for demonstration purposes only. Depending on the vehicle’s customization options, software version, and market region, the information displayed on the vehicle’s infotainment screen may differ from the information displayed in the owner’s manual. All directions referenced in the content (such as left, right, front, and rear) are based on the vehicle’s driving direction.
The vehicle’s functions will continue to improve and software updates will be frequently released to improve the user experience and functionality of the vehicle. Any major updates made after the print deadline will be reflected in the digital Owner Manual that can be accessed and viewed at   https://om.vinfastauto.com/
Copyright & Trademarks
All rights reserved. All content within this Owner Manual is subject to copyright and other intellectual property rights of VinFast. This documentation should not be copied, modified, or reused in anyway without prior written permission.
Important Notes
High Voltage Battery System
High-voltage cables are usually orange for easy identification. Do not touch, disassemble, or repair any high-voltage components on your own.
It is recommended to recharge the battery when the State of Charge (SOC) drops below 20%, and to charge it immediately when the SOC falls below 5%. Do not allow the high-voltage battery to become fully discharged (0%), as this may reduce its lifespan and performance.
Battery capacity will still decrease even when the vehicle is not in use. Ensure that the battery level remains above 30% if the vehicle will not be used for more than 2 weeks; the recommended range is 30% to 60%.
It is recommended to charge the battery fully at least once a month.
Vehicle Care
Perform regular maintenance, software updates, and technical updates (if any) as recommended by VinFast to ensure the vehicle remains in optimal condition.
When washing the vehicle, maintain a safe distance between the high-pressure water nozzle and the vehicle surface. Do not spray directly into the charging port, radars, sensors, electronic control units, or high-voltage components.
The vehicle should be inspected immediately after any moderate to severe collision, flood, fire, or other incidents.
Avoid driving through deep water, which may damage the battery or other components.
If towing is required, contact VinFast or a professional towing service. Use a flatbed tow truck to ensure all four wheels are off the ground.
Use of Genuine Parts and Accessories
Replace with genuine parts/accessories. Using non-genuine parts/accessories may affect vehicle safety and performance.
Flood Precautions
It is not recommended to drive in flooded roads or areas.
If it is mandatory to drive through a flooded area, to prevent the vehicle from being damaged, it is necessary to pay attention to the following precautions:
Check water depth before driving through water. The water level should not exceed the vehicle’s ground clearance.
Drive at a low speed (less than 10 km/h). Otherwise, waves may form in front of the vehicle, damage the motor controller and other components, and make the vehicle unable to drive.
Do not park or reverse in water under any conditions.
WARNING
Driving through deep water can damage your electrical motor and the high-voltage battery. This damage is not covered by the warranty.
Other systems like transmission, driving and electrical may also be seriously damaged upon submersion. This damage is not covered by the warranty.
Floodwater at or above the vehicle’s ground clearance can enter the high-voltage system and create serious hazards. Servicing flood-damaged vehicles by unauthorized workshop cannot guarantee, please contact VinFast for support and bring vehicle immediately to VinFast authorized dealers for checking and repair if vehicle is flooded.

# Overview


## Vehicle Overview

Electric Vehicle Overview
Your vehicle is powered by electricity and as a result some of the vehicle’s systems and operating characteristics are different from a vehicle powered by an internal combustion engine.
It is important to carefully review this entire owner’s manual for this reason. The vehicle does not require, and it is not capable of using gasoline like a vehicle powered by a traditional internal combustion engine.
The vehicle uses two types of batteries. One is a 12-volt (low voltage) battery that is the same as the battery in vehicles that operate on gasoline powered engines, the other is the LFP battery which may be referred to as the High Voltage (HV) or Electric Vehicle (EV) battery in this Owner Manual. The 12-volt battery provides power to the vehicle systems and features such as the infotainment system, supplemental restraint systems, headlights, and windshield wipers.
The vehicle uses energy stored in the LFP battery. The vehicle’s LFP battery must be charged with electricity before the vehicle can be driven. As the vehicle operates, the LFP battery gradually discharges. If the LFP battery becomes completely discharged, the vehicle will not operate until it is re-charged.
The high voltage LFP battery or EV Battery provides power to the electric motor that drives the vehicle.  The vehicle must be plugged in to an appropriate charging point for the LFP battery to be charged.
Your vehicle is also equipped with a regenerative braking system which allows the vehicle to convert kinetic energy into electrical energy, which is used to recharge the battery increasing overall range.
This vehicle is considered to be an environmentally friendly vehicle because it does not emit exhaust gases, such as carbon dioxide and nitrogen oxide when in operation.

# Openings & Compartments


## Keys and Locks

Central Locking Overview
The vehicle is equipped with a central locking system, which can be used by passengers inside the vehicle to control the locking/unlocking of all doors. Central locking and unlocking of the vehicle can be accomplished by any of the following:
Vehicle key fob
Keyless entry (Ensure that the smart key is within 1 meter of the driver’s door or the front passenger door.)
Mechanical key
Interior central locking button
Remote locking feature within the VinFast App (if available)
Interior Central Locking button
The central locking button   is located on the driver’s side door. The button will illuminate to indicate the vehicle is locked.
WARNING
When there are children in the vehicle, the central locking system should be used to lock the doors and prevent them from accidentally touching the interior door handles and opening the doors.
CAUTION
To ensure the normal operation of the central locking function, please try to avoid pressing the central locking button multiple times in a short period of time.
Automatic speed lock function
The vehicle is equipped with a Speed Sensing Auto Lock function. When enabled, all doors will automatically lock when the vehicle speed exceeds the configured threshold speed.
You can set the automatic locking function on/off and the locking speed on the Infotainment screen, see >   Settings > Vehicle Settings  .
Collision unlock function
In the locked state of the central locking system, if a serious accident occurs in the vehicle, the doors will automatically unlock, making it convenient for rescue and passengers to quickly exit the vehicle.
Automatic re-lock function
If the doors are unlocked via the remote key or passive entry button but no door is opened within 30 seconds, the vehicle will automatically lock all doors.
Key Fob Overview
Key Fob
The Key Fob is paired with the vehicle and uses Radio Frequency (RF) signals to perform the following vehicle functions:
Remote Lock/Unlock.
Find the car/Panic alarm.
Auto Up/Down power window.
Open/close Power Tailgate.
Starting the vehicle.

Remote control: Lower windows and unfold side mirrors (Auto Fold ON)
For a description of all Key Fob functions, see >   Remote Key Functions  .
WARNING
When exiting the vehicle, the key fob should be taken with the user. If the key fob remains in the vehicle and children are present, they can start the vehicle or operate the windows which can cause serious injuries or even death.
CAUTION
Protect the key from impact, high temperatures, and damage from liquids. Avoid contact with solvents, waxes, and abrasive cleaners.
NOTE
Please ensure that the vehicle is successfully locked before leaving.
If the remote control key does not work, it may be due to the battery running out of power and the battery needs to be replaced.
When using the remote key, please do not be too far away from the vehicle, otherwise the vehicle will not receive the remote key signal.
Key Fob antenna
The vehicle key fob communicates with the vehicle through an RF antenna. Key Fob communication with the vehicle may be impacted by the following:
The Key Fob battery is discharged.
The Key Fob is outside of the vehicle antenna operating range.
Interference caused by signals from powerful radio transmitters, mobile telephone, or electronic devices.
Interference as a result of nearby metallic objects.
WARNING
If a vehicle occupant has a pacemaker or other electrical medical implant/device fitted, they should maintain a safe distance as prescribed by their medical professional from the antennas in the vehicle. This is to avoid any possible interference between the vehicle antenna and the medical implant/device. See approximate vehicle Key Fob antenna locations below.
Using mechanical keys
The mechanical key can be used to unlock the vehicle door when either the key fob battery or the 12V vehicle battery is depleted.
To remove the mechanical key:
Press and hold the mechanical key switch   on the key fob.
Pull the mechanical key   out in the direction of the arrow.
CAUTION
The mechanical key body is relatively small. When not using the mechanical key, please do not remove it randomly to avoid loss.
Key Fob Battery
The battery will slowly deplete over a period of time even if the key is not being used. The following symptoms may exist to indicate that Key Fob battery is low:
There will be no response from the key fob or from the vehicle even though the key fob is within vehicle antenna range.
A vehicle alert will be displayed on the Infotainment screen.
To Replace the key fob Battery:
Remove the mechanical key.
Use a small flat tool to gently open the back cover   of the key fob.
Remove the internal battery cover   by first twisting anti-clockwise and pulling up the cover.
Replace the battery   and ensure the new battery is in the correct orientation (Battery Number: CR2032).
Reinstall the internal battery cover, press down and rotate clockwise to lock.
Reinstall the key fob back cover ensuring it clicks into place.
Insert the mechanical key back into its slot.
WARNING
Please keep the battery out of reach of children. The replaced battery is very small and, if accidentally swallowed, may cause serious injury or even death.
CAUTION
When changing the battery, do not touch any of the circuitry on the remote key. Static from your body could damage the remote key.
Incorrect disposal methods for waste batteries can damage the environment and endanger human health.
Do not crush, cut or burn the battery. Do not expose the batteries to environments wit extremely low air pressures or extremely high temperatures.
NOTE
If the Key Fob is damaged or does not function correctly even after replacing the battery, contact a VinFast Service Center.
Key Fob replacement
In case the key is accidentally lost or damaged, a replacement key will need to be paired with the vehicle. Please contact a VinFast Service Center for assistance.
Remote Key Functions
Vehicle Unlocking/Locking
Lock vehicle: When all doors, tailgate and front hood are closed, single press the lock button   to lock vehicle and activate the anti-theft.
Unlock vehicle: When the vehicle is locked, single press the unlock button   to unlock vehicle and disarm the anti-theft.
Open/Close the tailgate: Long press the tailgate button   to open/close the tailgate.
Find your car: Short press the panic button   can activate the car search function.
Panic alarm function: Long press the panic button   can activate the panic alarm function.
Key controlled window up/down function: When vehicle is in the OFF/ACC state, Single press, then press and hold the unlock button   on the remote key to lower all windows; long press the lock button   on the remote key to raise all windows.
Key controlled mirror fold/unfold function: When the Auto Fold function is ON, long press the unlock button   on the remote key to unfold the side mirrors; long press the lock button   on the remote key to fold the side mirrors.
Passive Entry
The passive entry feature allows the vehicle to be conveniently accessed without using the remote key. After pressing the button on the outside handle of driver’s door or front passenger’s door, the central locking system will verify the presence of valid remote key and will lock or unlock the vehicle.
To lock/unlock the vehicle via Passive Entry Button:
Ensure that you have the key fob with you (within 1m of the driver driver’s door or passenger’s door ). Press the Passive the passive entry button   on the door handle to lock or unlock the doors.
Locking the doors: Press the Passive Lock/Unlock button on either the driver’s door or the front passenger door to lock all doors.
Unlocking from the driver’s door: Press the Passive Lock/Unlock button on the driver’s door. The doors will be unlocked according to the vehicle settings.
Unlocking from the front passenger door: Press the Passive Lock/Unlock button on the front passenger door to unlock all doors.
To open/close the windows using the Passive Entry Button:
Ensure that you have the key fob with you (within 1m of the driver’s door or front passenger’s door ). Long press the passive entry button   to open/close all windows together with central locking or unlocking.
NOTE
For the vehicle to recognize the presence of the remote key and activate the passive functions, the key needs to be within a range of 0.8m to 1m from the driver’s door or passenger’s door.
Lock/Unlock Feedback
When the vehicle is locked or unlocked, it will give a confirmation signal through the off, lights, the horn, or both.
You can set the lock/unlock feedback through the infotainment screen, see >   Settings > Vehicle Settings  .
NOTE
If the lock switch is pressed when any of the doors are not fully closed, the vehicle will not be locked. The turn indicator will not blink indicating that all doors require to be checked to ensure they are correctly closed.
Unlock door options
You can set the unlock the driver door/all door options through the infotainment screen, see >   Settings > Vehicle Settings  .
Remote control window lowering/raising control
When vehicle is in the OFF/ACC state, ingle press, then press and hold the unlock button on the remote key to lower all windows, releasing the unlock button will stop the window lowering action.
When vehicle is in the OFF/ACC state, long press the lock button on the remote key to raise all windows, releasing the lock button will stop the window raising action.
Forgotten key reminder
When the driver door is closed and a key is left in the vehicle, a message will appear on the screen and an audible tone will sound to alert the driver. If the key is left in the vehicle, the doors cannot be locked.
Find your car
When the vehicle is locked and the remote key is within approximately 15m of the vehicle, short press the panic button on the remote key to activate the vehicle horn and turn indicator lights to help locate the vehicle.
When the Find your car function is active, press any button on the remote key again to cancel this function.
Panic alarm
When the vehicle is locked and in a stationary position, long press the panic button on the remote key to activate the panic alarm. The hazard lamps will flash and the horn will sound continuously.
When the panic alarm is active, press any button on the remote key will cancel this function.
Automatic re-lock function
If the door does not open after the vehicle is unlocked, the vehicle will lock again after a period of time.
Mechanical Key
The Mechanical Key is integrated into the vehicle key fob. The mechanical key can be used to unlock the vehicle door manually if the key fob battery or the vehicle’s 12 V battery is discharged.
To remove the mechanical key:
Press and hold the mechanical key switch   on the key fob.
Remove the mechanical key   in the direction of the arrow.
CAUTION
The mechanical key body is relatively small. When not using the mechanical key, please do not remove it randomly to avoid loss.
Unlock the door using the mechanical key:
Pull the driver’s door handle outward to the maximum angle and hold it.
Insert the mechanical key   into the driver’s door lock  .
Rotate clockwise to unlock the door.
NOTE
In the event of a drained 12V battery, use mechanical key to unlock the driver’s door.
If the vehicle is unlocked using the mechanical key while the key fob battery is depleted but the vehicle electrical system is still operating normally, the anti-theft alarm system will be activated. Start the vehicle promptly after unlocking to deactivate the alarm system.
Locking the Door Using the Mechanical Key:
Pull the driver’s door handle outward to the position where the mechanical key can be inserted into the key cylinder, and hold it there. At the same time, push the door inward so that it latches at the first catch position.
Insert the mechanical key   into the driver’s door lock  .
Turn the mechanical key counterclockwise to lock the door.
Remove the mechanical key.
Push the door fully closed to complete the locking operation.
NOTE
If the 12V battery is discharged, only the driver’s door can be locked using the mechanical key.

## Doors

Exterior Door
To open the door using the exterior door handles
Ensure the vehicle is unlocked via the key fob or passive entry button﻿.
Pull the handle outward while also pulling open the door.
NOTE
When the doors are opened, the indicator light for the door opening will be displayed on the infotainment screen.
Passive Entry
The Passive Entry feature allows convenient access to the vehicle without using the remote key buttons. Simply keep the key fob with you and within 1 meter of the driver’s door or passenger’s door. When you press the button on the outside door handle, the central locking system automatically detects the valid key fob and locks or unlocks the vehicle accordingly.
To unlock/unlock the vehicle via Passive Entry Button:
Ensure that you have the key fob with you (within 1m of the driver driver’s door or passenger’s door ). Press the Passive the passive entry button   on the door handle to lock or unlock the doors.
Locking the doors: Press the Passive Lock/Unlock button on either the driver’s door or the front passenger door to lock all doors.
Unlocking from the driver’s door: Press the Passive Lock/Unlock button on the driver’s door. The doors will be unlocked according to the vehicle settings.
Unlocking from the front passenger door: Press the Passive Lock/Unlock button on the front passenger door to unlock all doors.
To open/close the windows using the Passive Entry Button:
Ensure that you have the key fob with you (within 1m of the driver’s door or passenger’s door ). Long press the passive entry button   to open/close all windows together with central locking or unlocking.
WARNING
Ensure that the doors are correctly closed and secure in order to prevent inadvertent or sudden door opening, potentially resulting in an accident that may lead to serious injury or death.
Ensure no objects or body parts extend outside the vehicle while in motion.
Interior Door
Use the inner buckle handle to open the door from the inside. All doors are equipped with buckle handles.
When the vehicle is in locked, pull the inner buckle handle twice in a row to open the door.
When the vehicle is unlocked, pull the inner buckle handle once to open the door.
NOTE
When the doors is opened, the indicator light for the door opening will be displayed on the Infotainment screen.
Child Rear Door Lock
The Child Rear Door Lock prevents the rear doors from being opened from inside the vehicle, enhancing rear occupant and child safety. When this lock is engaged, the rear doors can only be opened by using the exterior door handle.
To activate/ deactivate Child Rear Door Lock:
Open the rear door.
Locate the child lock positioned on inside edge of the rear door.
To activate or deactivate the child lock:
Slide the child lock switch inward in the direction of Arrow  , as illustrated, to activate the child lock.
Slide the child lock switch outward in the direction of Arrow  , as illustrated, to deactivate the child lock.
WARNING
Do not leave children unattended in the vehicle.
Ensure all doors are closed and locked prior to driving.
Always engage the child lock when traveling with children in the rear seats to prevent them from opening the rear doors when the vehicle is in motion, which may result in serious injury or death.
NOTE
The child lock feature does not lock the doors from the outside.

## Front and Rear Compartments

Liftgate
The liftgate is located at the rear of the vehicle, and provides access to the rear cargo compartment. Liftgate opening conditions:
The liftgate is securely closed
The vehicle has sufficient battery
The gear is in Park (P) and the vehicle is stationary
Power Liftgate Operation
The Liftgate can be operated using any of the following methods:
Key Fob
Liftgate control button on Touchscreen
Interior and Exterior Switches
VinFast App (if available)
Emergency Interior Switch
To open/close the Power Liftgate using the key fob:
Long press the liftgate button   on key fob once to open or close the power liftgate.
NOTE
The turn indicator lights flash three times when the tailgate is opened or closed.
To open/close the Power Liftgate using﻿ touchscreen:
Touch targets will appear on Car Control Area if the vehicle is in Park.
Tap to unlock the liftgate lock icon  . Once the liftgate is opened, the icon will change to the liftgate unlock icon  .
To open/close the Power Liftgate via the exterior and interior switches:
The Liftgate can be operated using the liftgate switches present on the exterior and interior of the vehicle.
Press the exterior switch   to open the liftgate.
Press the interior switch   to close the liftgate.
To stop auto liftgate movement:
The power liftgate movement can be stopped using the following methods:
Press the interior liftgate switch.
NOTE
The liftgate is fitted with Anti-Pinch Sensors. If any obstacles are detected while closing the liftgate, it will revert to open position again.
Liftgate Height Adjustment
To adjust height of Power Liftgate:
Open the liftgate fully.
Press and hold the liftgate switch   for 3 seconds until a beep is heard.
Set the liftgate to the desired height.
Press and hold the liftgate switch   again for 3 seconds until beep is heard to confirm the desired height.
Close liftgate with button.
The liftgate will then open to the set position.
WARNING
Do not allow children to operate the liftgate button.
Do not allow passengers to travel in the rear cargo compartment area.
Do not keep the liftgate open, while the vehicle is in motion.
When opening or closing the liftgate, check for any obstacles (pedestrians or objects) around the liftgate to prevent damage to the trunk or objects and pedestrian injury during the opening or closing process.
Do not leave children unattended around an open luggage compartment. They could get inside the luggage compartment and accidentally close the liftgate trapping themselves inside.
In colder regions, remove all the snow which can accumulate before opening the liftgate otherwise liftgate may get closed automatically due to the weight of the snow causing serious injuries.
CAUTION
Ensure that the liftgate is closed properly. When correctly closed, the liftgate should be flush with all surrounding panels.
Emergency Safety Release:
If the electric liftgate switch does not operate, the liftgate can be operated manually from inside the vehicle. The mechanical switch of the liftgate is located on the inside of the vehicle’s liftgate.
Enter the rear space and flatten the backrest of the rear row seats, see >   Seats > Seat Adjustment  .
Remove the inside cover   on the back of the liftgate.
Push and hold the switch, as shown above  . Then push the liftgate to open.
CAUTION
If both the remote key and the vehicle systems are working properly, but the liftgate can only be opened manually (Emergency Safety Release), there may be a malfunction in the liftgate system. Please visit a VinFast Service Center to have your vehicle inspected.
Front Hood
The hood provides access to the﻿ 12V battery, front compartment fuse box, and fluid/coolant reservoirs. See >   Fluids, Lubricants and Parts > Fluids, Lubricants and Parts Overview  .
To open the front hood
Pull the front hood release handle   under the driver’s dashboard twice to release the hood latch.
Stand in front of the vehicle and lift the hood to its fully open position.
Remove the support rod   from the clip and insert it into the designated hole on the hood, making sure it is securely and correctly positioned.
WARNING
Ensure that the area near the hood is clear of obstacles before opening.
Make sure the hood support rod is properly positioned and securely fixed into the designated hole to prevent the hood from falling, which could result in serious injury or even death.
Do not drive the vehicle with the hood raised or not fully latched. This can block your vision and cause accident.
Always close the hood completely and securely before driving the vehicle.
To close the front hood
Lift the hood slightly, remove the support rod from the designated hole, and secure it in the retaining clip.
Lower the hood slowly until the first position of the lock is engaged.
Push down on the hood to engage the second stage lock.
CAUTION
Do not install accessories other than original VinFast products on the hood. This may increase the weight of the hood and cause the hood support to fail.
Ensure that the area near the hood is clear of obstacles before closing. To prevent damage:
• Do not apply excessive force when closing.
• To prevent dents caused by concentrated force in one area, close the hood using both hands.
• To prevent the possibility of creasing do not apply excessive pressure to the front edge of the hood.
• To avoid scratches, make sure your hands are empty while closing hood.

## Windows

Window Controls
The vehicle windows can be controlled using the window switches, which are located on the driver’s side door and the passenger side doors.
Driver’s Side Door:
Driver Window Switch
Front Passenger Window Switch
Rear Right Passenger Window Switch
Rear Left Passenger Window Switch
Passenger’s Side Door:
To open and close the windows:
Locate the window control switch on the driver’s side front door or passenger side doors.
Slightly push the switch forward to open the window.
Slightly pull the switch backward to close the window.
Release the switch at any time to stop window travel﻿.
Auto-Opening/Closing (Express Mode)﻿:
To fully open the window automatically, push the window switch forward completely and release it, the power window will open until the window reaches the lowest position.
To fully close the window automatically, pull the window switch backward completely and release it, the power window will close until the window reaches the highest position.
WARNING
Before closing a power window, ensure that all occupants, especially children, do not have any body parts extended through the window opening. Failure to do so could cause serious injury.
While closing the window, check for obstructions like a head, hand, etc., Which may lead to personal injuries.
For safety reasons, always switch on the safety lock, it can prevent children from accidentally using the power windows. Do not allow children to play with the power windows.
CAUTION
To avoid electrical overheating of the switch, window operation is temporarily disabled if the windows are repeatedly operated in a short period of time.
Avoid tinting the window glass as a tinted window can affect visibility of rear-view mirrors.
NOTE
The driver’s side window control switches have the main priority over the other passenger window switches.
Do not open or close two windows or more at the same time to prevent possible damage to the power window system. This will also ensure long life of the fuse.
Initialize Windows:
If the windows cannot be closed automatically via the express function (e.g. after disconnecting the vehicle 12V battery or after replacing the Anti-Pinch Module fuse), the windows may need to be re-initialized.
Preconditions:
Vehicle is in: ACC, ON (Driver present)
Gear is P
Vehicle speed less than 3 km/h
To re-initialize windows:
Operate switch to raise windows to the top position.
Operate switch to raise windows again, causing the windows to stall upwards at the top position for a duration of 2 seconds.
Operate switch to lower windows to bottom position.
Operate switch to raise windows to top position.
NOTE
Steps in above mentioned procedure should be successfully completed in a single attempt. In case of any interruption, repeat the procedure from the beginning.
Remote control window lowering/lifting control
The windows may also be opened via the Key Fob, See >   Remote Key Functions  .
Safety Functions
Child Window Lockout
The child window lockout prevents passengers from operating the windows by disabling the window control switches on the front and rear passenger seats. This feature helps improve safety by preventing accidental window operation and reducing the risk of injury while the vehicle is in motion.
WARNING
Always activate the child window lockout to prevent children from opening the front and rear windows and reduce the risk of falling out of the vehicle.
To activate/deactivate the Child Window Lockout:
The child window lockout button is located on the Driver View area of the Infotainment screen. When activated, the front passenger and rear passenger power window switches are disabled and cannot be operated from the passenger seats.
Tap the window lock button  , the power window will be locked.
Tap the window unlock button  , the power window will be unlocked.
NOTE
Ensure the child window lockout is enabled by checking the function using the front passenger and rear passenger power window switches.
Anti-Pinch Safety Function
If an obstacle is detected while the window is closing, the window will automatically stop and move down slightly.
If the window detects an obstacle repeatedly while closing, the Express Window Up function will be disabled. Users need to move the window to the fully closed position manually to restore the automatic function.
Express Window UP function will not function if the anti-pinch function is detected multiple times. Complete the window initialization process to regain the express window function. See >   Windows > Window Controls  .
Overload Protection
To avoid electrical overload, the window operation is disabled for a short period of time if the switch is repeatedly operated at short intervals.

## Vehicle Security

Theft Alarm
The theft alarm system helps to protect the vehicle and its contents from break in. When activated the system will emit both an audible and visual alert using the vehicle horn, turn signals.
Once the theft alarm system is activated, it will continuously monitor the status of the vehicle’s door locks. When it is detected that the correct remote key has not been used to open the door/liftgate, the theft alarm system will be triggered.
If the theft system malfunction indicator light on the central control screen lights up, the vehicle cannot activate the theft alarm system normally. Please contact the VinFast service center in a timely manner.
The vehicle thief alert will trigger in following situations:
When vehicle anti-theft is activated, any of the front or rear doors are illegally opened.
When vehicle anti-theft is activated, liftgate or hood is illegally opened.
If the vehicle location is different from the saved location.
When the anti-theft alarm has been triggered, the vehicle horn will sound and the turn indicators will flash.
Theft Alert Operation
To activate the theft alert:
The theft alert system is activated when the vehicle is successfully locked and all doors, hood, and liftgate are properly closed.
NOTE
When all vehicle doors, hood and liftgate are closed, locking the vehicle can activate the anti-theft alarm.
To change the alert type:
You can set the type of anti-theft alarm through the infotainment screen, see >   Settings > Security Settings  .
ON duration:
The anti-theft alarm will activate for 28 seconds, pause for 10 seconds, repeat this cycle 10 times, and then stop completely. The turn indicators and horn will be active for the same period.
GPS Monitoring
When GPS Monitoring is enabled and the vehicle is in Secured state, the system periodically checks vehicle location. If the vehicle is moved unexpectedly, the theft alarm may be triggered. This feature will be deactivated on the next start.
To turn ON/OFF GPS monitoring system:
Tap App Library   on the touchscreen.
Or, Slide down from top of touchscreen to open Notification Center.
Tap Settings   > Security Settings   > Theft Alert.
Toggle ON/OFF on GPS Monitoring.
Deactivating the Theft alarm system
You can use any of the following methods to disarm the anti-theft alarm:
Unlock/Lock the vehicle again by the remote key.
Unlock/Lock the vehicle again by the passive entry button (with a valid remote key around).
Open tailgate by the tailgate button on the remote key or the tailgate exterior button (with a valid remote key around).
Press the panic button on the remote key.
Start the vehicle with a valid remote key inside the vehicle.
NOTE
If the key fob battery is completely discharged, then the alarm will not be deactivated by pressing the key fob Unlock button. To disable the alarm when the key fob battery is discharged, place the key in the center console armrest box area (emergency start area), see >   Starting & Shutting Down Vehicle > Starting the Vehicle  .
Some anti-theft functions require a valid remote key to be detected near or inside the vehicle.
Immobilizer
The immobilizer is an electronic security device that prevents the vehicle from being started with an invalid or incorrect key fob. The immobilizer functions automatically without the need for manual activation or intervention.
Vehicle Immobilizer Operation
The immobilizer is automatically activated when the vehicle is in OFF state.
Each key provided with the vehicle is equipped with a specific coding responder. Only with the correctly encoded key can the vehicle be started. The immobilizer indicator will be displayed briefly on the infotainment screen when starting the vehicle. If the immobilizer system recognizes the key fob correctly, the indicator light will turn off.
If however the system does not recognize the coding of the key fob, the indicator light will flash. This may prevent the vehicle from starting. If this occurs please ensure you have the correct key fob in the vehicle, remove any metal objects around the key which can cause interference. If this repeatedly occurs and inhibits the vehicle from starting, it is recommend to contact a VinFast Service Center.
WARNING
The vehicle has anti-theft function, but it cannot prevent all theft activities and cannot guarantee the absolute safety of the vehicle.
Do not tamper with electrical devices in the vehicle as this can cause terminal vehicle issues.
NOTE
If there is electromagnetic interference around the vehicle, such as power plants, signal towers, etc., it may cause the anti-theft system to malfunction.
If the infotainment screen displays indicative information about the anti-theft system malfunction, it indicates that there is a malfunction in the anti-theft system. Please contact the VinFast Service Center in a timely manner.

# Seats, Restraints & Safety


## Seats

Front Seat Overview
A correct seating position is very important to ensure the safety of the driver and passengers. Follow these recommendations for proper seating positions:
Please follow all seating guidelines to ensure optimum protection during an accident. Seats, headrests, seat belts, and airbags work together to ensure occupant safety.
Before driving the vehicle, it is necessary to adjust the seat to the correct position to ensure optimal protection in the event of an accident. The seat, headrest, seat belt, and driver airbag work together to ensure the overall safety of driver and passengers.
WARNING
Do not adjust the driver’s seat while the vehicle is moving. Adjusting the driver’s seat while the vehicle is in motion may cause loss of vehicle control and may lead to an accident resulting in serious injury or death. Always adjust the driver’s seat only when the vehicle is stationary.
Ensure to maintain the safety distance from the steering wheel and airbag when adjusting the seat.
NOTE
Ensure to sit in the correct seating position to ensure optimum driving comfort, see >   Correct & Incorrect Seating Positions  .
Use of a seat belt is mandatory for the entire duration of the journey.
Rear Seat Overview
Please follow all seating guidelines to ensure optimal protection during an accident. Seats, headrests, seat belts, and airbags work together to ensure passenger safety.
NOTE
Ensure to sit in the correct seating position to ensure optimum driving comfort, see >   Correct & Incorrect Seating Positions  .
Use of a seat belt is mandatory for the entire duration of the journey.
The rear seats are designed in a 60-40 configuration. The rear seats can be folded individually or together to increase cargo capacity when needed, see >   Seat Adjustment  .
The rear ﻿center seat also contains an integrated armrest along with two cup holders, see >   Cup holders  .
Seat Adjustment
The vehicle is fitted with a driver’s seat and four passenger seats. Properly adjusting the seats helps maximize occupant safety while driving.
WARNING
Under no circumstances should the vehicle have more occupants than the total seats. Traveling with an excessive number of passengers is dangerous to all occupants, even to those who may be safely restrained.
During vehicle operation, it is prohibited to adjust the driver’s seat, otherwise danger may occur.
Sitting in a fully reclined position when the vehicle is in motion can be dangerous and reduces the effectiveness of vehicle safety systems. This increases the chance of serious or fatal injuries in the event of a collision or sudden stop.
All occupants in a moving vehicle must keep the seat backs upright and fasten the seat belts at all times.
Ensure to keep fingers clear of moving seat components while adjusting to avoid risk of injury.
The driver and front passenger should sit as far rearward as possible to reduce risk of injury from a deploying airbag.
Do not put any luggage, loose items or pets on the front passenger seat.
Do not adjust the seats while driving as this may lead to an accident and cause serious injury.
Do not allow children to play inside the vehicle when the rear seats are folded as this may lead to serious injury.
Make sure hands, fingers, feet or other body parts are kept clear of the seat folding mechanism while adjusting or folding the rear seat.
Ensure each rear seat backrest is locked securely in position to ensure the backrest does not move during sudden braking or during an accident.
Do not put any heavy or unsecured luggage on the rear seats or inside the passenger compartment.
When returning a seat backrest back to its upright position, make sure it is firmly locked.
Driver Seat Adjustment (6-way power)
Forward/Rearward Adjustment:
Slide the adjustment button   forward or rearward to adjust the seat to the desired position.
Seat cushion high/low adjustment
Pull/push the rear section of the adjustment button   upward or downward to adjust the seat cushion up/down.

Pull the adjustment lever   and simultaneously pull the seat upward until locked in position.

When returning the rear seat backrests to the upright position, ensure that the seat belt is not caught behind the seat.
Before adjusting the rear seat backrests the vehicle should be parked on level ground with the parking brake applied.
Do not put heavy items on the seats when they are folded as they may damage the folding mechanism.

WARNING
Ensure the driver’s seating position is adjusted prior to driving. An incorrect seating position may impact both driving comfort and the ability for the driver to control the vehicle.
Check seat surroundings before adjusting seat position to avoid injury to other passengers or damaging the seat.

Correct & Incorrect Seating Positions
A correct seating position is very important to ensure safety to driver and passengers. Follow these recommendations for proper seating positions:
The driver and passengers feet must be on the floor.
The driver and passenger should not slide forward on the seat.
The driver and the front passenger should maintain a distance of from the steering wheel or the dashboard.
The backrest must be in an upright position and the occupants back should be resting against it.
The backrest should not be reclined to a laying down position when the vehicle is in motion.
Ensure that all occupants are sitting at the ﻿center of each designated seat. Sitting in a haphazard manner (offset from the seat ﻿center, sideways, etc.) may cause fatal injury in the event of an accident.
Children should not lean against the dashboard area.
The driver’s arm should be just slightly bent (around 120 degrees).
All occupants of the vehicle must wear seat belts when the car is in motion for the entire duration of the journey.

## Seat Belts

Seat Belt Use
Wearing seat belts correctly in the event of a collision can reduce personal injury and protect the head neck and chest of drivers and passengers from injury.
WARNING
Each seat belt is only allowed for one person to use, and it is not allowed for multiple people (including children) to share the same seat belt.
Do not change, disassemble or install equipment that may change the direction or tension of the seat belt.
After any collision, the vehicle should contact the VinFast Service Center to check the seat belt system. Even if the seat belt does not appear to be damaged, some of its internal protective functions may no longer operate. If the seat belt is found to be damaged or running abnormally, it must be replaced.
Introduction and use of seat belt types
The vehicle is equipped with three-point seat belts for all seating positions. The front seats and the two outboard rear seating positions are fitted with shoulder belt pretensioners and emergency locking retractor (ELR). The front seats are also equipped with a pyrotechnic lap pretensioner (PLP). The rear center seating position is fitted with an emergency locking retractor (ELR) only and is not equipped with a pretensioner.
When a vehicle experiences a serious frontal collision, the pre-tensioner works together with the airbag, automatically retracting the seat belt, eliminating the slack in the seat belt, thereby reducing forward movement of passengers.
When the seat belt tensioner is triggered, it will release a small amount of dust (smoke) and may make a loud noise. This is a normal phenomenon and will not cause a fire. The smoke and dust generated during prolonged exposure to the detonation of the tensioner may cause skin or eye discomfort.
When a passenger experiences forward movement that causes the seat belt to reach a certain tension, if the front seat belt tensioners are triggered, the force applied to the diagonal section of the seatbelt will be limited in order to help reduce the chest compression injury.
WARNING
Once the seat belt tensioner is activated, it must be replaced.
If the seat belt has burrs, contamination, or damage, it must be replaced in a timely manner.
Correct seating position
A correct seating position is very important to ensure the driver’s and passenger’s safety. Follow all recommended seating guidelines to minimize the chances of injury in the event of an accident.
For more information, see >   Seats > Correct & Incorrect Seating Positions  .
WARNING
If the occupants of the front seats are close to the steering wheel or Infotainment display, the airbag system may not provide optimal protection, which increases the risk of injury or death.
To fasten a seat belt:
Latch Plate
Buckle
Ensure a correct seating position.
Advance the seat belt from its retracted position. Make sure that the belt is not twisted and lays flat across the occupant’s body (shoulders, torso, and pelvic region), as shown  .
Insert the latch plate into the buckle  , to make sure that the latch plate clicks in to locked condition.
Pull the seat belt to check that it is fastened securely.
Pull the diagonal part of the seat belt over the occupant’s torso and shoulders towards the retractor to remove any excess slack  .
WARNING
Inadequately positioned seat belts may increase the risk of serious injury in an accident. To avoid wearing the seat belt incorrectly, occupants must always sit in the correct seating position; place one arm under the shoulder belt and the other over the shoulder belt (ensure that the shoulder belt is not against your neck or face) and place the lap belt across the hip (as low as possible), not across the waist.
Ensure that the seat belt fits the body snugly and the belt is not twisted and the latch plate is locked properly into the buckle.
Do not place other items between the seat belt and the body.
Do not use a fake seat belt tongue.
Please keep the seat belt buckle clean and promptly remove any foreign objects inside the buckle. If you are unsure how to proceed, please contact the VinFast Service Center.
To unfasten the seat belt:
Grasp the seat belt latch   to prevent rapid retraction of the seat belt after it is released, which may cause injury to passengers or damage to the vehicle.
Press the “red” seat belt buckle button   to unlock the seat belt. When not in use, place the seat belt properly.
If the seat belt is not fully retracted, manually retract it and do not leave the seat belt in a loose and hanging state.
Testing Seat Belts
Make sure each seat belt operates correctly by performing these three simple checks.
After fastening the seat belt, pull the seat belt upwards to ensure that the seat belt buckle can securely lock the seat belt latch.
Unroll the webbing to the limit with the belt unfastened. Make sure that the unreeling is free from snags and that the webbing is in good condition. Check that the webbing retracts smoothly and completely.
Grasp the seat belt latch and quickly pull the seat belt forward. It should automatically lock and prevent further pulling out.
If a seat belt fails any of these tests, contact a VinFast Service Center immediately.
Wearing a Seatbelt while Pregnant
Pregnant women must wear their seat belts correctly when riding in vehicles, which is crucial.
The seat belt must be worn comfortably, and the waist belt should be pulled down as much as possible to wrap around the lower part of the waist instead of the waist. The shoulder belt should pass through the shoulders and chest, and the shoulder belt or waist belt of the seat belt should not pass through the abdomen. Please consult a doctor for specific guidance.
WARNING
Pregnant women should avoid using seat belts that touch the raised abdomen. The waist belt should be flat against the thighs and placed as low as possible below the abdomen.
Do not wear twisted seat belts. Excessive forces will be transferred from the belt to the occupant in a collision, resulting in serious personal injury.
Seat belt Warning Indicator
Driver and Passenger seatbelt status:
The seat belt warning system is used to remind passengers inside the car to fasten their respective seat belts before driving.
When a seat is occupied and the corresponding seat belt is not fastened, the seat belt warning light   will illuminate on the Infotainment screen.
When the vehicle is started or the speed is less than 15km/h, and there are passengers who have not fastened their seat belts, the infotainment screen will light up a visual warning icon; When the vehicle reaches a speed of 15km/h or more, the seat belt warning system will also emit an audible warning signal and the visual warning icon will blink. After the alarm lasts for 95 seconds, the warning sound will automatically disappear and the visual warning icon will stop blinking but remain lit until you fasten your seat belt.
The reminder device alerts passengers through the following methods:
The seat belt unfastened icon on the infotainment screen.
Sound reminder signal.
WARNING
Seat belts must be used by all occupants whenever the vehicle is moving. Not wearing seat belts may result in serious injury or death.
Do not unfasten a seat belt while driving for any reason. Failure to follow this warning could result in serious personal injury or death, in case of accident.
If a seat belt is damaged or the retractor mechanism is not working properly, get the parts replaced by VinFast Service Center. Using a damaged seat belt is very dangerous and may result in serious personal injury or death.
Do not modify or tamper with the seat belt or its mechanism as it may lead to failure of the system. Using a seat belt with damaged component is very dangerous and may cause serious injury or death.
Do not latch the seat belt into the buckles of other seats. Seat belts are designed to function effectively only when the latch plate of a particular seat belt is locked into its paired buckle. Failure to follow this warning could result in serious personal injury or death.
Do not wear the shoulder belt under your arm or behind your back. Doing so is unsafe and may result in serious personal injury or death.
Do not wear the seat belt with any part of the strap twisted. A twisted seat belt will not function properly in an accident and could result in serious personal injury or death.
Multiple people should not use a single seat belt. Seat belts are designed to protect a single occupant occupying a particular seat where it is mounted/attached.
Make sure there is nothing in the buckle interfering seat belt’s latch plate. This may prevent the seat belt from fastening securely. Failure to follow this warning could result in serious personal injury or death.
CAUTION
Do not wear a seat belt over fragile, hard, or sharp objects. This can damage the objects or seat belt.
Seat Belt Care
A seat belt is a critical component within the vehicle occupant protection system. Proper care must be taken to ensure that they function effectively in the event of an accident or a collision.
Do not use defective or damaged seat belts. Please visit the nearest VinFast service center as soon as possible to check your seat belt.
Recommendations for seat belt care:
Ensure that the seat belt is not damaged or clamped by sharp objects.
Always keep the seat belt retractor clean and free of dust.
Avoid contamination of the seat belt webbing by polish, oil, chemicals, and particularly battery acid.
Seat belts can be cleaned lightly using mild, non-abrasive soap and lukewarm water. After washing, clean the seat belts thoroughly. Do not allow the belts to retract until they are completely dry.
Regularly inspect the seatbelt webbing for contamination, damage, or fraying.
Do not bleach or dye the webbing because this may weaken the seat belt.
Do not insert any foreign items such as coins, clips, etc., into the seat belt buckles. If foreign materials get into a seat belt buckle, the seat belt may not work properly.
WARNING
Do not disassemble, modify, or tamper with the seat belt system, as it may affect the seat belt performance.
In case of accident, a seat belt system may not provide adequate protection.
CAUTION
Do not use bleach, strong detergents, or an abrasive brush or cloth to clean the seatbelt as it may damage and weaken the fabric.
Do not use steamers, brushes with hard bristles, hard sponges etc to clean the seats as this can damage the seats.

## Child Restraint Systems

Child Restraint Systems Overview
The seat belts fitted in the vehicle are designed to fit and secure adults and older children. Small children and infants must be secured in a certified Child Restraint System (CRS) suitable for their age, height, and weight, and for the seat position of choice. Not all CRS are safe to use in all seat positions.
When installing a Child Restraint System (CRS), use the following seating positions in order of preference: the outboard seats in the second row, followed by the center seat in the second row. The front passenger seat should only be used when absolutely necessary and only where permitted by the laws and regulations of the country or region in which the vehicle is operated.
Most countries will have laws and regulations which stipulate children need to travel in an approved child restraint system. Please be aware of the requirements in the country where the vehicle is driven.
Before installing a child safety seat on the front passenger seat, it is necessary to disable the front passenger airbag. The front passenger airbag can be disabled through the button on the infotainment screen. Notification via the   icon is displayed on the infotainment screen.
To Turn Off Front Passenger Airbag:
Swipe down from the top status bar of the infotainment screen to open the notification center, then tap the   button to turn off the front passenger airbag. A confirmation message will appear. Read the message content and confirm or cancel.
WARNING
Children should always be properly restrained in the rear seats of the vehicle.
In an exceptional circumstance, ensure Passenger Airbag OFF   when a child restraint system seat must be secured in the front passenger seat.
Children who are resting against, or very close to an airbag when it inflates can be seriously injured or killed.
Do not modify the child restraint system in any way.
An incorrectly secured child restraint system may cause serious or fatal injury to the child in the event of an accident. Always follow the manufacturer’s instructions to ensure the correct fitment of a child restraint system.
Do not use a child restraint system to secure more than one child.
Always ensure that a child is properly secured at all times. Failure to do so may result in the child being propelled from the child restraint system risking serious or fatal injuries.
Never hold an infant or child while riding in a vehicle.
Before installing a child restraint system, ensure that the seat backrest is in an upright position and locked.
Never leave children unattended in the vehicle, even if they are secured in a child restraint system.
A seat belt or a child restraint system may become hot when exposed to sunlight. Take care before fastening a child to avoid burns.
Laws and regulations that govern how children should be restrained in a vehicle are subject to change. Therefore, it is the driver’s responsibility to be familiar and comply with regulations of the region where the vehicle is driven.
The image below identifies where child restraint systems can be fitted in the vehicle. Please read all the information in the following section to identify the correct type and orientation when securing children in the vehicle.
NOTE
Regulations regarding the installation position of child seats may vary depending on the market, region, or country where the vehicle is operated. Please always comply with local laws and safety regulations.
Types of Child Restraint Systems:
There are three types of child restraints systems suitable for fitment to your vehicle categorized by their orientation and/or design:
Rear Facing
Forward Facing
Booster Seat
Each of these restraint types may be mounted using one of the following fixture methods:
Belted-secured via the vehicle’s seat belt
ISOFIX / i-Size - secured via anchor points built into the vehicle’s seat frame.
A summary of both restraint and fixture methods is summarized below:
Rearward Facing Child Restraint System:
Rearward facing child restraint systems are designed so the child is facing towards the rear of the vehicle when driving. They give the best support and protection to a child’s head and neck in the event of a collision. All children below the age of one should be seated in a rearward facing child restraint system. Continue to use a rearward-facing child restraint system for as long as a child fits within the height and weight limits permitted by the child restraint system manufacturer.
Forward Facing Child Restraint System:
Forward facing child restraint systems are designed so the child is facing towards the front of the vehicle when driving. In a forward-facing child restraint seat, a three point harness incorporated in child restraint system secures the child. Keep a child in a forward-facing child restraint system until they reach the height or weight limit permitted by the restraint system manufacturer. Once a child exceeds the recommended height and weight of a forward-facing child restraint system a booster seat is recommended.
Booster Seats Child Restraint System:
A booster seat child restraint system is used when a child exceeds the height or weight limit of a forward-facing child restraint system. Booster seats are designed to work in conjunction with the vehicle’s existing seat belt system. A booster seat positions a child’s body to ensure correct routing of a seat belt across the shoulder and lap areas, avoiding contact with the face, neck, and stomach.
A child should remain in a booster seat until they exceed restraint system recommendations.
WARNING
Always read and follow the instructions provided by the manufacturer of the Child Restraint System.
Failure to follow all warnings and instructions could increase the risk of the serious injury or death if an accident occurs.
Do not install a booster seat or a booster cushion with only the lap strap of the seat belt.
Do not install a booster seat or a booster cushion with a seat belt that is slack or twisted.
Do not put the safety seat belt under a child’s arm or behind their back.
Do not use pillows, books or towels to boost a child’s height.

Forward-facing child restraint system for small children in 9 to 18 kg weight class.

Rear-facing child restraint system for children of maximum size in the weight class up to 18 kg.
D - ISO/R2
Rear-facing child restraint system for small children in the weight class up to 18 kg.
E - ISO/R1
Rear-facing child restraint system for young children in the weight class up to 13 kg.
F - ISO/B2
Forward-facing booster seat for children of maximum size in 9 to 18 kg weight class.
NOTE
ISOFIX is an international standard that specifies the attachment points for child safety seats in passenger cars.
Selecting the Appropriate Child Restraint System
Select an appropriate child restraint system based on a child’s height and weight.
Check if the child restraint system to be installed is compatible with the vehicle type.
Ensure that the seating location in the vehicle is suitable for your child restraint system.
Select a child restraint system that fits the vehicle seating position where it will be used.
NOTE
Always read and follow the instructions for installation as specified by the child restraint system manufacturer. If in doubt, contact a professional to complete the installation process.
Always secure the child restraint system properly with a seat belt or remove it from the vehicle when it is not in use.
A child restraint system which has been subjected to stress in an accident must be changed immediately before next use.

Front Passenger Seat Fitment
WARNING
Ensure Front Passenger Airbag is turned OFF when a CRS is fitted to the front passenger seat.
Laws and regulations that govern how children should be restrained in a vehicle are subject to change. Therefore, it is the driver’s responsibility to be familiar and comply with regulations of the region where the vehicle is driven.
Refer to the following label located on the sun visors:
With Front Passenger Airbag is turned OFF, a forward facing child restraint system may be fitted on the front passenger seat.
The following instructions must be followed when fitting a child restraint system on the front passenger seat:
Position the child restraint system on the front passenger seat ensuring its base is pressed firmly against the seat back.
Avoid any gap between the child restraint system and passenger seat back by adjusting the seat back vertical position.
Position the base of the passenger seat as far rearward as possible.
For a child restraint system in groups 2 and 3, make sure the guide fitting attached to the child restraint system headrest is positioned in front of or level with the seat belt D-ring attached to the vehicle body (B-pillar).
Check that the belt routing through the guide is free of kinks and not bent over the edge of the guide fitting.
Rear Passenger Seat Fitment
A child restraint system may be fitted to both the rear outboard and rear center seats depending on fixing type. Refer to the child restraint system overview to determine suitability of seat type in each rear seat location.
See >   Child Restraint Systems > Child Restraint Systems Overview  .
Rear Outboard Fitment - Belted Type
Fully extend the seat belt to its maximum length to engage the automatic locking retractor (ALR).
Buckle the seat belt around the child restraint seat in accordance with the child seat manufacturer’s instructions.
Allow the seat belt to retract and remove all slack in the belt by pushing the child restraint seat into the vehicle’s seat.
If the child restraint seat is equipped with a top tether, attach it to the anchor point located at the rear of the back seat. See top tether section below.
CAUTION
When fitting a rearward facing child restraint system in the rear outboard seat, move the front seat forwards or adjust it’s backrest to ensure no interference between the child restraint system and the front seat.
ISOFIX / i-Size Type
The vehicle has been provided with ISOFIX﻿ anchors, which allows compatible child restraint systems to be attached directly to the rear seat structure, rather than using a seatbelt. ISOFIX metal arms on the child restraint attach and lock onto a U-shaped steel bar at the rear of the seat cushion.
The ISOFIX anchor points can be identified by ISOFIX markings   found on both rear outboard seats.
Extend the child restraint system latches onto the anchor bars until they click into place. Carefully read and follow the instructions provided by the child safety seat manufacturer.
Always test the safety seat before seating a child. This can be done by pulling the child restraint system away from the seat and moving it from side to side. It is critical that both latches are fully locked in position.
The rear seat cushion has a flap at the iso-fix area. To install the CRS, this flap must be opened
The following is an example of a rear facing ISOFIX attached child restraint system.
Rear Center Fitment -Belted Type
Children can occupy the rear center passenger seat in a forward facing position only, secured using a belted child restraint system.
NOTE
The rear seats are the safest location for a child to travel.
Support Leg (if equipped)
If your Child Restraint System is equipped with a support leg, ensure that it is fully extended and secured to the floor of the vehicle. Support legs can only be used in the rear outboard passenger seat locations. Follow installation instructions as described by the CRS manufacturer.
WARNING
When using a child restraint with a support leg, be sure that it rests firmly on the floor.
Top Tether Straps and Anchor Points
The top tether anchor is located at the back of the rear seats of the vehicle.
Installation of top tether anchors:
For single strap tethers - Route the tether strap from in between the headrest stems and attach it to the tether anchor at the back of the seat or follow the CRS manufacturer installation instructions.
For dual strap tethers - Route the tether straps provided with the child restraint system on either side of the headrest and attach them to the tether anchor at the back of the seat or follow the CRS manufacturer installation instructions.
WARNING
Please follow the instructions provided by the child restraint system manufacturer to ensure the child is seated safely and securely.
Adjust the seat harness for every child for each vehicle journey.
Make sure that the tether strap does not have any slack or twist.
Always inspect the seat belts, child restraint system belts and tether straps for damage and wear.
Never attach any equipment, harnesses, or other items to the anchor points in the vehicle.
Do not use a forward-facing child restraint system until the child is over 9kg (20 lbs).
The tether straps must be attached to only the tether anchor point and nowhere else. Tighten the tether strap according to the instructions provided by the manufacturer.
Never attach two child restraint systems to one anchor point as it may be incapable of securing both seats in an accident.
Only use anchorages and top-tethers of seats on which the CRS is being installed. Otherwise, poorly installed CRS may become detached from the seat causing serious injuries or death.
Before installing a child restraint system, be sure to confirm that the seat back is fully upright and securely locked into place. Otherwise, in an accident, serious injury or death could result.
Never use a child safety seat that has been involved in an accident. Have the seat inspected or replaced as described in the child safety seat manufacturer’s instructions.
Do not use seat belt extenders on a seat belt that is being used to install a child safety seat or booster seat.
Do not hold babies or infants on a passenger’s lap. All children must be securely restrained in the vehicle using a child restraint system.
Never leave children unattended inside the vehicle.

## Airbags

Airbags
The vehicle is equipped with Supplemental Restraint System (SRS) airbags and seat belts for the driver and passengers. The airbags are designed to provide maximum protection in conjunction with fastened seat belts.
WARNING
Always wear a seatbelt since it maybe the only restraint system protecting the occupant as airbags by design may not necessarily deploy in every vehicle collision.
Airbag Locations
Passenger Airbag
Front Seat Side Airbags (Left & Right)
Curtain Airbags
Driver Airbag
How Airbags Work
The airbag system consists of:
Airbag modules.
Occupant Classification System (OCS)
Crash sensors, monitoring system, and airbag indicator   (shows the operational status of airbags).
The Supplemental Restraint System constantly monitors multiple vehicle sensor inputs to determine the severity of a vehicle collision and decide which airbag should deploy, if required. An airbag will inflate rapidly with force when activated accompanied by a loud noise.
It is possible that airbags may not deploy as a result of minor or underbody collisions or impacts with narrow objects. There can be significant superficial damage to the vehicle without resulting in airbag deployment and conversely, relatively small structural damage can trigger the airbags to deploy. Therefore, the external appearance of the vehicle after a collision is not indicative of whether or not the airbags should have inflated.
WARNING
Inflatable airbags and properly fastened seat belts limit the driver’s mobility and reduce the risk of injury from contact with vehicle internal components.
Always wear seat belts properly at all times even when an airbag system is provided. Failing to wear a seatbelt can cause serious injury or death.
Do not sit unnecessarily close to any airbag deployment zone. The driver should maintain a recommended distance from the steering wheel while still maintaining control of the vehicle.
Do not place objects, stickers or mount equipment/accessories on or near the airbag deployment zones. Failure to follow these instructions may increase the risk of injury or death in the event of an accident.
To ensure that the Airbag system will operate correctly, never place your feet on the dashboard, outside the window, or on seat surfaces.
Various components of the Airbag system are located throughout vehicle. Incorrect work or repairs could damage the airbag system components. As a result, in the event of an accident, the airbags may deploy incorrectly or won’t deploy altogether, causing serious or fatal injuries.
The Airbag system is only able to provide protection during a single collision. A crash can damage the Airbag systems in the vehicle. A damaged Airbag system may not properly protect you and your passenger(s) in a crash, resulting in serious injury or even death. To help make sure the Airbag systems are working properly after a crash, contact VinFast Service Center and have them inspected and any necessary replacements made as soon as possible.
Do not use unauthorized accessory seat covers, they could prevent Airbag deployment and increase risk of injury in an accident.
Do not lean your head on the door. You could risk getting injured from the deployment of airbags from the side of the seat back.
Ensure that all passengers are seated upright, centered on the seat cushion with a seatbelt correctly fastened, legs extended with feet on the floor. Failure to do so could result in serious injury upon airbag deployment.
Do not modify the vehicle. It may affect the functioning of the Airbag system. Contact a VinFast Service Center before doing any modifications to your vehicle. Any unauthorized modifications to the vehicle may cause serious damage to critical systems and endanger occupant’s lives.
If the front passenger airbag is not deactivating either when a child restraint system is fitted or the seat is unoccupied, there may be a fault with the Occupant Classification System. Failing to follow this warning may cause serious injury or death of the occupant.
All children of age 12 years and below are recommended to occupy the rear seats only. Failing to follow this warning may cause serious injury or death.
It is not recommended to install a child restraint system on the front passenger seat even if legally permitted. If however, a child restraint system must be fitted in this location, ensure the front passenger airbag OFF icon is present and remains illuminated at all times on the Infotainment screen. Fitment of a child restraint system in the front passenger seat with an active airbag can cause serious injury or death in an event of airbag deployment.
NOTE
During a period of time after the deployment of the airbag, the vehicle’s high-voltage battery will disconnect from the electrical system, rendering the vehicle inoperable.
When an airbag is deployed, powder like dust particles may be released into the vehicle cabin. This is not an indication of a vehicle fire. The fine dust may cause skin or eye irritation and could cause breathing difficulties. To avoid such effects leave the vehicle when safe to do so if able or alternatively open windows or doors. It is advisable to wash your hands and face with a mild soap and water to reduce effects.
Some collisions may appear to have significant structural damage but the airbags may not deploy. Conversely, some collisions with lower levels of structural damage may trigger an airbag deployment. An airbag will deploy if the vehicle deceleration level is high enough in order to provide the necessary protection. Therefore, the appearance of the vehicle after a collision does not indicate whether the airbag should have inflated or not.
Passenger Front Airbag Status
When the front passenger seat airbag is OFF,   icon will be displayed at the Infotainment screen.
You can slide down from the top status bar of Infotainment screen, tap   button turn on/off front passenger airbag.
The Impact of Expansion
When the airbag expands, it will release a fine powder. This powder may irritate the skin and should be thoroughly rinsed off eyes and cuts or abrasions.
If the airbag expands or the vehicle experiences an accident, it is necessary to inspect the airbag, seat belt tensioner, and any related components; Please contact the VinFast Service Center or an approved dealer for inspection or replacement.
When a collision occurs, in addition to the airbag expanding, the doors will also be unlocked accordingly.
Airbag Warning Indicator
The airbag indicator light   on the screen is used to indicate whether there is a malfunction in the airbag system.
Normal Condition -  The indicator light will light up for a few seconds and then turn OFF after the vehicle is started.
Abnormal Condition - The indicator light to illuminate or flash continuously.
NOTE
If the abnormal condition is noticed, stop the vehicle and park it in a safe location and contact the VinFast Service Center.
Types of Airbags
Front Airbags
The vehicle is fitted with both a driver and passenger front airbag. The driver’s airbag is located in the center of the steering wheel and the passenger airbag is located in the dashboard area. Both airbag deployment locations have “AIRBAG” identifiers.
How the airbag is triggered:
The driver and front passenger airbags are designed to deploy during significant frontal collisions, minimizing the risk of injury to the head and shoulder region.
During a collision the vehicles crash sensors will receive information regarding the collision type, the collision angle, and the severity of impact. Based on this information the vehicles Supplemental Restraint System (SRS) will determine if the crash event meets the criteria for front airbag deployment.
WARNING
Make sure that nothing is in between the passenger and airbag deployment area as it may interfere with airbag deployment.
Avoid leaning against the door or the console area while vehicle is in motion.
The airbags are part of the supplementary restraint system and are designed to accompany the seatbelt in protecting the occupants from injury in the upper body. The airbags inflate rapidly so maintaining a proper seating position is of utmost importance to prevent incurring injury once an airbag deploys.
NOTE
In the event of a rear or side collision, the front airbags may not deploy, and in this case, only the front seat belt tensioner will activate.
Side Airbags
The vehicle is equipped with side airbags to protect the driver and front passengers. There are two side airbags located on the outermost side of each front backrest. These airbags protect passengers in the event of a side collision or rollover. The airbag deployment locations have “AIRBAG” identifiers.
How the side airbags are triggered:
During a collision the vehicles crash sensors will receive information regarding the collision type, the collision angle, and the severity of impact. Based on this information the vehicles Supplemental Restraint System (SRS) will determine if the crash event meets the criteria for side airbag deployment.
Due to the minimal distance between the passenger and the door, the side airbags will inflate rapidly. Side airbags are designed to reduce the risks of injuries to the head and upper body.
WARNING
Do not keep any objects near airbag deployment zones or near the side door glass as it may cause a serious injury or death when airbags deploy.
Do not bang, kick, or hammer the side of the vehicle when the vehicle is on. Such actions may cause side airbags to deploy.
CAUTION
Do not open or repair any parts of the side airbag system, as this may disrupt its functioning. If necessary, have the system serviced by a VinFast Service ﻿Center.
NOTE
Side airbags may not be deployed in the event of a rear end collision. Both front seatbelt pre-tensioners however will be activated.
