Safety seat belts and the vehicle airbag system will provide protection only when occupants are correctly seated.
Curtain Airbags
The vehicle is equipped with two curtain airbags mounted in the roof headliners above the doors. Curtain airbags deployment locations have “AIRBAG” identifiers, located on the door pillar. They are designed to protect passengers in case of severe side impacts, vehicle roll-overs or offset frontal crashes.
How the curtain airbags are triggered:
During a collision the vehicles crash sensors will receive information regarding the collision type, the collision angle, and the severity of impact. Based on this information the vehicles Supplemental Restraint System (SRS) will determine if the crash event meets the criteria for curtain airbag deployment.
The curtain airbags help to reduce the risk of injury to the head and neck area of the occupants.
WARNING
Avoid attaching any object near the area which covers the deployment zone of the curtain airbags.
Ensure that the passengers are seated close to the back of their seat with a correctly fastened seat belt to obtain the best protection from the curtain airbag.
The vehicle with smaller wheelbase have a significantly higher rollover rate than other types of vehicles. To prevent rollovers or loss of control:
• Take corners at slower speeds.
• Avoid sharp turns and abrupt maneuvers.
• Do not modify vehicle in any way that would raise the center of gravity.
• Keep tires properly inflated.
At highway speeds, you are much more likely to rollover if you lose control of your vehicle. The driver may lose control if two or more wheels fall off the road and the driver have to oversteer to regain control.
In a rollover crash, an unbelted person is significantly more likely to die than a person wearing a seat belt. Ensure all passengers are wearing their seat belts.
CAUTION
In case of a severe side impact collision, the curtain airbags will deploy even if there are no occupants seated.
Do not open or repair any parts of the side curtain airbag system, as this may disrupt its functioning. If necessary, have the system serviced by a VinFast Service Center.
Operation & Occupant Detection
Occupant Detection Status
The vehicle is fitted with an Occupant Classification sensor that automatically establishes if an adult or a child is occupying the front passenger seat, adjusting the airbag status, and the airbag deployment accordingly. If an adult occupies the seat the passenger airbag will be ON. When a child or a Child Restraint System (CRS) is seated in the front passenger seat, the front passenger airbag can be manually switched OFF using the touchscreen.
Please follow the recommendations to ensure correct occupant detection:
Sit in an upright position in the seat with shoulders and back against the seat back and legs extended in front with feet on the floor, See >   Seats > Correct & Incorrect Seating Positions  .
Fasten the seatbelt.
Remain seated correctly at all times.
The following situations can cause an inaccurate occupant detection status:
Placing large or heavy items on the front passenger seat.
Placing powered electrical devices which may interfere with the front passenger seat occupant classification sensor.
Incorrectly fitting a CRS system.
Items located under the seat or wedged between the cushion and seat back.
Any ﻿unauthorised accessories fitted to the seat.
WARNING
If the front passenger seat airbag status is not turning ON or OFF as expected when the seat is occupied, do not allow any passengers to occupy the seat until it has been inspected and rectified by an ﻿authorised VinFast Service ﻿Center.
To ensure accuracy of the Occupant Detection System, do not modify the front seat in any way.
If it is legal in your region to have a child seated on the front passenger seat, ensure the passenger airbag is not active. Otherwise it could cause serious injury or death if the airbag gets deployed in case of an accident.
It is the drivers responsibility to ensure the passenger airbag status is OFF if a child is occupying the front seat.
When the front passenger seat airbag is OFF,   icon will be displayed at the infotainment screen.
NOTE
It is recommended that all children 12 years and under must be seated in the rear seats of the vehicle.
Airbag Deployment Conditions
Driver and Front Passenger Airbags
Vehicle is equipped with sensors. In the event of vehicle collision these devices calculate the impact on the vehicle and at a certain impact the airbag get triggered/deployed. Other factors also related to density and stiffness of the impacted vehicle or objects are also considered.
The driver and front passenger airbags protect the head, neck, and chest of the driver and front passenger, preventing direct impact of the steering wheel/dashboard. The airbag will only expand in the event of a serious frontal collision.
Front Seat Side Airbags
The front seat side airbags will deploy between the passenger and the door trim, protecting the passenger’s chest, shoulders, and buttocks and minimizing injuries.
Curtain Airbags
The curtain airbags will deploy from the roof area along the side windows, protecting passengers in case of severe side impacts, vehicle roll-overs or offset frontal crashes.
WARNING
Do not install or place any accessories (cup holders, box holders, stickers, etc.) on the steering wheel or dashboard where the airbag is installed, as these items may cause personal injury or death if the airbag is deployed.
Do not place any objects in the area between the outer side of the seat and the door trim panel, as it may cause personal injury or death when the airbag is deployed.
Avoid attaching any object near the area which covers the deployment zone of the curtain airbags.
Do not use seat covers on the backrest of the front seats, as seat covers may interfere with the deployment of the front seat side airbags, causing personal injury or death.The airbag system can only provide one-time accident protection function. If the airbag has been triggered, the airbag system components must be replaced.
When the airbag is deployed, High voltage battery will be disconnected. Please contact the VinFast Service Center or Customer call center immediately for assistance.
Airbags Inflate Condition:
In the event of a frontal and side collision, when the vehicle’s sensors detect sufficient collision intensity, the airbag will deploy.
The airbag may deploy if bumps or objects on uneven roads or sidewalks affect the vehicle chassis.
Situations Where Airbags May Not Deploy
In many types of accidents, such as rear end collisions, side collisions, oblique collisions, special rear end collisions, rollover accidents, and certain special collisions, the airbags may not deploy.
In secondary or tertiary collisions, or low-speed collisions, even if the airbags are deployed, they cannot provide additional protection.
The vehicle collided with a pillar or tree, but the collision force did not meet the triggering conditions of the airbag sensor.
Due to the collision force and deceleration not meeting the requirements, the vehicle does not meet the triggering conditions of the airbag sensor when it collides with other vehicles or enters the lower part of a large vehicle.
When a rear end or side collision causes the vehicle to roll over, the airbags may not deploy.

# Advanced Driver Assistance Systems (ADAS)


## Driving Assistance

ACC - Adaptive Cruise Control
Adaptive Cruise Control (ACC) is an advanced driver assistance system that automatically adjusts vehicle speed to maintain a safe following distance to the vehicle in front. The system uses a combination of radar and forward facing camera to maintain a safe and comfortable distance to the vehicle in front. ACC can stop and auto-resume with time bound in congested traffic. ACC will maintain a user selectable safe distance based on TimeGap from a forward vehicle, or maintain a chosen set speed.
ACC will reduce the vehicle’s speed if it is determined to be too high while driving through a curve.
Objects that Adaptive Cruise Control can detect and brake:
Cars
Trucks
Buses
Motorbikes (not all types)
Bicycles (not all types)
NOTE
ACC does not brake for animals, pedestrians, bumps and oncoming or crossing vehicles.
Adaptive Cruise Control Operation
ACC Controls Overview:
Activate ACC
Resume button
Cancel button
Distance gap decrease button
Distance gap increase button
Push up: Increase speed
Pull down: Decrease speed
To set Adaptive Cruise Control﻿:
To engage ACC, users can push the gear shift lever one step downward.
When ACC activated: Set speed   will display with blue color and the leading vehicle will be highlighted (if detected).
NOTE
At first ACC activation cycle if there is no target detected in front, the vehicle needs to be between the speed of 20 km/h and 150 km/h to activate Adaptive Cruise Control function.
At first ACC activation cycle if there is a target vehicle then the ACC can be activated at any speed below 150 km/h.
In the next ACC activation cycle the ACC can be activated at any speed below 150 km/h with or without a target.
* Maximum Limit as mentioned, is the feature of system, customer is liable to follow laws of the land as applicable while driving.
To resume ACC to a previous set speed:
After first ACC activation, if the driver deactivates ACC using Stalk switch​   (moving the gear shift lever inward), or by pressing the brake pedal, ACC can be resumed again by using the Stalk switch​   (moving the gear shift lever outward) with memorized settings of set speed and time gap. It is allowed at any speed within the range less than 150km/h or without leading vehicle as long as the driver confirmed.
To adjust ACC speed:
To adjust the set speed, use the left scroll wheel on the steering wheel.
To increase the set speed, push the left scroll wheel   upward.
To decrease the set speed, pull the left scroll wheel   downward.
To set new speed- override by accelerator pedal and push the stalk switch down.
NOTE
Single push or pull of left scroll wheel   will increase or decrease the speed by 1 unit
Push or pull and hold the left scroll wheel    for increasing or decreasing the speed by 5 units
The minimum set speed is 20 km/h and the maximum is 150 km/h.
* Maximum Limit as mentioned, is the feature of system, Customer is liable to follow laws of the land as applicable while driving.
To adjust the distance gap to the vehicle ahead:
To increase the distance gap, press the right button  .
To decrease the distance gap, press the left button  .
NOTE
Vehicle distance gap will default to the maximum setting after the first vehicle start, or to the driver’s saved preference for subsequent starts.
The ACC system will be cancelled by:
Moving the right stalk switch inward  .
Pressing the brake pedal.
When the driver remains in override mode more than 15 minutes.
When the vehicle speed is > 150km/h (93 mph).
System fault.
ACC Stop & Go Operation
During Stop & Go operation, if the vehicle has stopped due to detecting stationary traffic ahead, the driver must manually resume ACC when stopped for 3 seconds or more. If the stop is under 3 seconds the system will automatically resume operation.
Additional considerations:
If the vehicle ahead is stationary, the ACC system may hold the vehicle for up to 5 minutes. The driver can resume ACC at any time.
The driver must press the throttle pedal or using right stalk switch in order to resume ACC to follow the leading vehicle, if the holding time is between 3 seconds to 5 minutes and the leading vehicle takes off during this holding time.
If the vehicle is required to hold for more than 5 minutes, the electronic parking brake will automatically apply and the driver will need to release the parking brake or press the throttle pedal to release it. Once the parking brake is released, ACC can be reactivated by moving the right stalk switch outward.
CAUTION
If front camera and radar are obstructed or not available, ACC will not be available for operation​
Due to the system limitations, the ACC may not be able to detect stationary objects, two wheelers which are partially into the vehicle path. The driver must stay alert at all times and takeover vehicle control as necessary.​
The user must always monitor vehicle behavior and the road ahead when ACC is active and always be prepared to take full control of the vehicle. When ACC is activated, The user is always required to control the steering wheel when ACC is active.​
NOTE
If equipped with Driver Monitoring System (DMS) that is camera based and driver detected that he pays attention, and the vehicle stop is under 5 minutes, the system shall automatically resume operation.
Adaptive Cruise Control can be used in combination with Speed Limit Control Function, see >   Driving Assistance > ISA - Intelligent Speed Adjustment  .
Adaptive Cruise Control Modes
Adaptive Cruise Control (ACC) automatically maintains the selected vehicle speed and the driver-selected following distance from a vehicle ahead. Depending on the driving situation, ACC provides the following operating behaviors.
Follow Mode:
When a slower vehicle is detected ahead, ACC automatically adjusts the vehicle speed to maintain the selected following distance.
If the leading vehicle slows down, ACC reduces the vehicle speed as required. When the road ahead is clear, ACC gradually accelerates the vehicle back to the previously set speed.
If another vehicle cuts into your lane, ACC automatically adjusts the vehicle speed to re-establish the selected following distance.
The following distance can be adjusted at any time using the distance setting controls on the steering wheel.
Curve Speed Control
When driving through curves, ACC may automatically reduce vehicle speed to improve driving comfort and vehicle stability.
Once the road straightens and operating conditions permit, ACC gradually returns the vehicle to the previously set speed.
The amount of speed reduction depends on factors such as vehicle speed, road curvature, and driving conditions.
Overtaking Support
While following another vehicle, activating the turn indicator may indicate the driver’s intention to overtake.
If the system determines that conditions are appropriate, ACC may temporarily increase vehicle acceleration to assist the overtaking maneuver.
After the overtaking maneuver is completed, ACC automatically resumes normal speed and following distance control.
Cruise Control Mode
Maintains the driver-selected vehicle speed when no vehicle is detected ahead. The driver must remain attentive and brake or take control whenever necessary.
Under Cruise Control Mode, user can set minimum speed of 20 km/h and maximum speed of 150 kph while using Adaptive Cruise Control.
WARNING
Overtaking mode will operate only when suitable conditions are met. Always check vehicle lane markings and road conditions ahead prior to overtaking.
The driver is always responsible for vehicle speed and the distance to other vehicles ahead.
Always pay attention to the traffic around you even when adaptive cruise control is enabled.
ACC is prohibited to use under following conditions:​
In urban areas where traffic density is high with combination of multiple class of vehicles​
Roads with multiple successive curves.​
When the road surface is in poor condition.​
Slippery surfaces.​
Too bumpy surfaces​
Roads with steep slopes over 20%.​
When there is exit from expressways and around construction zones.​
While towing any vehicle.​
When there is bad weather (such as ice, fog, gravel, heavy rain but not limited to).​
WARNING
ACC performance may be reduced, or the system may not operate as intended, in the following situations:​
- In complex traffic or highly dynamic surroundings where vehicles, pedestrians, or other objects cannot be reliably detected or classified.​
- When pedestrians, vehicles, or other road users suddenly or rapidly move into the vehicle’s path.​
- When a pedestrian, vehicle, or other road user cannot be clearly distinguished from the background.​
- When driving through tight curvy road or during dynamic or aggressive driving.​
- When encountering large vehicles, such as trucks or buses, or vehicles with unusual shapes or configurations.​
- Always be aware of the set speed and vehicle distance gap​
​In complex driving situations, ACC may issue a warning or apply the brakes even when there is no immediate targets. Consider to use higher time gap
setting to follow the targets at higher speed which gives sufficient distance from front vehicles. Do not rely solely on ACC to prevent a collision. ​
Overtaking mode will operate only when suitable conditions are met. Always check vehicle lane markings and road conditions ahead prior to overtaking.​
TSR - Traffic Sign Recognition (if equipped)
The Traffic Sign Recognition System (TSR) assists the driver by notifying the current posted speed limit and other traffic signs. ﻿Traffic signs may be displayed on the Infotainment screen.
In addition, the system may also detect other non-speed related traffic signs. These may include:
Bicycle paths
Expressways
Town zone
Snow
Camping zone
Highways
Traffic sign icons can be displayed on Driver View Area. For more information, see >   Drive View > Overview  .
How Traffic Sign Recognition Works
Traffic Sign Recognition uses a camera mounted on the windshield together with recognition software that detects road traffic signs on the road ahead. The system then displays this information on the Infotainment screen. When Adaptive Cruise Control is active and the Intelligent Speed Adjustment is enabled via the Infotainment screen, the vehicle’s speed will be automatically adjusted as per the detected speed sign﻿. For more information, see >   Driving Assistance > ISA - Intelligent Speed Adjustment  .
Some examples of traffic signs that may be recognized depending on the country:
Traffic Sign
Display
Speed Limit
No Entry
Residential Zone
Highway Zone
Town Zone
End of Town Zone
Express Way
Bicycle Way
NOTE
Traffic Signs shown above are for reference only. The vehicle will detect and show traffic signs on the Infotainment screen based on the region of operation.
Traffic Signs may not be the same for all regions.
Traffic Sign Recognition System Limitations
TSR system performance may be impacted in the following conditions:
Poor visibility due to snow, fog, or heavy rain.
Poor visibility due to glare from oncoming traffic or the sun.
At high-speeds.
If the camera’s field of vision is obstructed; e.g., dirt or stickers.
If the traffic signs are completely or partially covered.
If traffic signs do not conform to the standard format.
If traffic signs are damaged or bent.
If traffic signs are displayed on sign holders with electronic signs.
If the traffic signs or the roadways have changed and the navigation data is no longer up-to-date.
To activate/deactivate Traffic Sign Recognition:
Tap  ADAS    in the Driver View area on the screen.
O r, Tap  App Library    >  Drive Aids  .
Tap  Driving Assistance    >  Speed.  Find  Traffic Sign Recognition (TSR) . Tap  ON/OFF  on the toggle button.
O r,   Tap  Quick Access   . Tap  TSR  to activate/deactivate this feature.
To activate/deactivate Speed Limit Warning:
Tap  ADAS    in the Driver View area on the screen.
O r, Tap  App Library    >  Drive Aids  .
Tap  Driving Assistance    >  Speed.
Find  Speed Limit Warning (SLW) .
Tap  ON/OFF  on the toggle button.
Tap on the chime toggle to Mute/Unmute audio chime (audible warning).
The driver can also apply a speed offset above a detected speed limit. Press + and - buttons to increase or decrease the offset in speed base.
WARNING
Vehicle speed always remains the responsibility of the driver. Always observe traffic signs while driving.
Under certain circumstances, traffic sign recognition may not detect traffic signs correctly or not detect at all. As a result, the system may not display the correct speed limit.
CAUTION
The speed limit sign displayed in Driver View Area is based on the units used (mph or km/h) in the country where the vehicle is being operated. This may differ from the unit of speed displayed on the vehicle’s speedometer, which is determined by the driver’s vehicle settings.
NOTE
The Traffic Sign Recognition system only displays the traffic sign icons on the Infotainment screen. It will not adjust vehicle speed. Please activate Intelligent Speed Adaption to enable this functionality.
For optimal performance of Traffic Sign Recognition  (TSR) , Speed Limit Warning (SLW), and Intelligent Speed Adjustment (ISA), especially in regions mandating intelligent speed assistance systems like the European Union, both GPS and Internet connectivity are required.
Note that if there is a loss of GPS and/or Internet connection, this may degrade the performance of these key functionalities or temporarily disable TSR, SLW, and ISA.
TSR, SLW, and ISA may not be available in all regions/markets. For detailed information on availability and coverage zones, please contact a VinFast dealership.
ISA - Intelligent Speed Adjustment (if equipped)
Intelligent Speed Adjustment (ISA) assists in controlling the vehicle speed within the legally enforced speed limits to assist safe driving.
When the system is activated in combination with Adaptive Cruise Control and Highway Assist, Intelligent Speed Adjustment will adjust the vehicle set-speed when there is a new detected speed limit after the driver confirms by pressing the ISA button on the steering wheel.
Since ISA is an assistance system, it will only assist the driver in adjusting the speed to the legal limits. The driver will always be responsible for controlling the vehicle speed within legal limits and should always be available to take control of the vehicle.
NOTE
This function can be used in combination with Highway Assist and Adaptive Cruise Control. This is not a standalone feature.
Traffic Sign Recognition must be ON in order to enable Intelligent Speed Adjustment.
For optimal performance of Traffic Sign Recognition (TSR), Speed Limit Warning (SLW), and Intelligent Speed Adjustment (ISA), especially in regions mandating intelligent speed assistance systems like the European Union, both GPS and Internet connectivity are required.
Note that if there is a loss of GPS and/or Internet connection, this may degrade the performance of these key functionalities or temporarily disable TSR, SLW, and ISA.
TSR, SLW, and ISA may not be available in all regions/markets. For detailed information on availability and coverage zones, please contact a VinFast dealership.
ISA may not work if the absolute delta (between TSR the speed limit value and the driver’s set speed) is more than 50 km/h.
To activate/deactivate Intelligent Speed Adjustment:
Tap  ADAS    in the Driver View area on the screen.
O r, Tap  App Library    >  Drive Aids  .
Tap  Driving Assistance    >  Speed.
Find  Intelligent Speed Adjustment (ISA) .
Tap  ON/OFF  on the toggle button.
The set speed icon will be shown on the touchscreen with a market-specific traffic sign indicating that ISA is now active.
Whenever a new speed limit value is detected, short press the left scroll wheel to adapt the set speed to the new speed limit.
System Malfunctions
A malfunction warning will be triggered when a failure is detected in the ISA system or with other related ADAS systems and will be shown on the Infotainment screen to notify the driver.
WARNING
Intelligent Speed Adjustment relies on speed limit information from the Traffic Sign Recognition system. The Traffic Sign Recognition system may not detect posted speed signs in poor weather or if the signs are poorly visible (not in the field of view) or the signs are in poor condition. The system will also be impacted when the front camera is blocked by dirt, snow, or ice; if they are in poor condition; or due to sun glare. The driver will always be responsible for controlling the vehicle speed within legal limits and should always be in available to take control of the vehicle.
Intelligent Speed Adjustment is a Driving Assist System. The driver has full responsibility to maintain the speed and control of the vehicle at all times.
Always be alert for vehicles or objects that may suddenly appear in front of you. If necessary, depress the brake pedal to reduce your driving speed in order to maintain a safe distance.
Always be aware of the set speed.
The vehicle may increase/decrease speed on a slope.
CAUTION
Always make sure that all cameras, sensors and radars are clean and in working condition.
Highway Assist (if equipped)
Highway Assist is a driver assistance function that supports the driver during highway driving and congested traffic conditions (traffic jam) supporting a safe and comfortable journey. Highway Assist adjusts the vehicle speed to match the flow of the traffic. It also adjusts the steering to keep the vehicle inside its lane boundaries.
NOTE
Highway Assist is designed to be used on highways and highway-like roads (roadways separated by constructional means) and it is the responsibility of the driver to use this function in appropriate road conditions.
Drivers are required to keep their hands on the steering wheel at all times while this feature is active.
This feature can be used in combination with Intelligent Speed Adjustment.
The driver must supervise the system under all conditions and is always ultimately responsible for control of the vehicle.
Preconditions for activating Highway Assist are:
Adaptive Cruise Control (ACC) conditions are fulfilled.
Speed range is within 0 km/h (0 mph) to 150 km/h (93 mph).
Two-lane markings or the leading vehicle are detected.
Lane width must be within the range of 2.5m and 5.5m.
The gear position should be in Drive (D).
Driver hands-on conditions are satisfied.
WARNING
Keep your hands on the steering wheel at all times when driving.
The Highway Assist function is designed to assist the driver in operating the vehicle and is not a fully autonomous driving system. Whenever a driver uses an assistive technology, he or she should always check to ensure conditions are suitable and if necessary, take appropriate measures to adjust vehicle speed or direction.
Ensure the road ahead is observed at all times and traffic laws followed.
In no event will the vehicle manufacturer be responsible for a driver’s actions that cause traffic violations or accidents.
The vehicle may drift to one side or may depart from the driving lane when it is driven at high speed or on a curve.
It is not recommended to use or activate this feature when towing anything such as a trailer, recreational vehicles, etc. Failure to comply may result in property damage, serious injury or death.
User shall take control of the vehicle when Lane markings are faded, missing, covered, or difficult to detect, Construction area , temporary lane markings, Roads have sharp curves,  complex interchanges, merging/diverging lanes, vehicle is driving through intersections, tollgate or exit/entry ramps.
Highway Assist Features
Highway Assist consists of the following main components:
Adaptive Cruise Control  - See >   Driving Assistance > ACC - Adaptive Cruise Control  .
Lane Centering Control - Assists in keeping the vehicle centered in its lane by applying small corrective steering inputs.
Auto Lane Change Assist (if available)- See >   Driving Assistance >  ALCA - Auto Lane Changing Assist .
Automatic Emergency Stop Assist  - See >   Driving Assistance > AESA - Automatic Emergency Stop Assist  .
When Highway Assist is active, the Traffic Jam Assist feature will also operate at lower vehicle speeds. Traffic Jam Assist is an extension of Adaptive Cruise control and is described later in this section.
Highway Assist Operation
To activate Highway Assist
Highway Assist Activation​
When Highway Assist is available, the lane fill and lane lines are displayed in gray,
indicating that the system is in Standby and ready for activation.​
To activate Highway Assist:​
Shift the vehicle into D (Drive).​
Ensure Highway Assist is in Standby and both lane lines are detected.​
Activate via the deep downward stalk movement  .
When Highway Assist is activated, the following indicators change to blue:​
Lane fill and lane lines, indicating that Highway Assist is actively assisting with vehicle steering control within the detected lane.​
Set speed icon   and trajectory display, indicating that Adaptive Cruise Control (ACC) has been automatically activated alongside Highway Assist.​
To adjust gap:
When a vehicle ahead passes in front of your vehicle or a slower vehicle is detected ahead, the vehicle speed will be adjusted to maintain the current gap setting. To adjust the gap setting:
To increase the distance gap, press the right button  .
To decrease the distance gap, press the left button  .
To adjust speed:
If you have switched the system ON, you can set or change the set speed using the left scroll wheel on the steering wheel.
To increase the set speed, push the left scroll wheel   upward.
To decrease the set speed, pull the left scroll wheel   downward.
NOTE
Press and hold the left scroll wheel   for increasing or decreasing the speed by large increments.
Highway Assist Temporary Degradation​
Highway Assist may temporarily enter Degraded Mode under certain conditions
When Highway Assist is in Degraded Mode:​
Steering assistance is temporarily unavailable.​
Adaptive Cruise Control (ACC) remains active and continues to control vehicle speed and maintain following distance.​
The driver must immediately take over steering control​
​The degraded state is indicated by:​
The lane fill and lane lines changing from blue to gray.​
The set speed icon and trajectory display remaining blue, indicating that ACC remains active.​
A short audible chime.​
When operating conditions permit, Highway Assist automatically resumes full functionality​
WARNING
During Highway Assist Degraded Mode, the driver is responsible for maintaining vehicle position within the lane at all times.
Highway Assist Deactivation​
Highway Assist can be deactivated at any time by driver action:​
Pressing the brake pedal.​
Performing the Cancel action   (by moving the gear shift lever inward).​
Highway Assist can also be automatically deactivated when operating conditions are not met (faults). When Highway Assist is deactivated:​
Both steering and speed assistance are canceled.​
The driver must immediately take over the driving task completely.​
A visual notification and audible warning may be issued to prompt the driver to take control.​
The lane fill, lane lines, and trajectory display change from blue to gray or are removed from the display.
Traffic Jam Assist
Traffic Jam Assist (TJA) is a low-speed (<60 km/h) operating mode of Highway Assist (HWA). It combines Adaptive Cruise Control (ACC) and Lane Centering Control that automatically controls vehicle speed and lane position based on traffic conditions ahead. The system helps alleviate some of the fatigue associated with driving in slow moving traffic that is constantly stopping and starting. Traffic Jam Assist uses both camera and radar along with steering wheel input to track a vehicle ahead and adjust both throttle and brake inputs to maintain a safe distance. The lane centering feature also ensures the vehicle remains safely in the center of its lane while maneuvering through congested highways and traffic jam.
Traffic Jam Assist does not require separate activation or deactivation; it automatically becomes available and disengages according to the activation and deactivation conditions of Highway Assist.
WARNING
Stay alert and prepare to take control of the car if the TJA system is deactivated.
Be sure to keep your hands on the steering wheel when you are using the TJA system.
It is the driver’s responsibility to always be aware of his or her surroundings when the system is operating, and be ready to take control of the vehicle at any time.
The steering wheel should be free of any objects (e.g. steering wheel covers) that could interfere with the hand detection sensor.
NOTE
If Traffic Jam Assist brings the vehicle to a complete stop for more than 3 seconds, the driver is required to either use the Resume button (by moving the gear shift lever outward) or press the accelerator pedal to resume operation.
If the vehicle remains stationary for longer than 5 minutes, TJA will be deactivated and the Electric Park Brake applied.
The function works with and without lane marking. As no lane marking is recognized, there must be leading vehicles and a virtual lane shall be created to guide the vehicle to follow.
AESA - Automatic Emergency Stop Assist
Automatic Emergency Stop Assist (AESA) is an emergency support function within Highway Assist (HWA). AESA is designed to bring the vehicle to a controlled stop in the same lane if the driver becomes unresponsive while the system is active.
AESA is not a standalone driving function. It supports the driver only during specific emergency conditions and does not replace the driver’s responsibility to remain alert and in control of the vehicle.
NOTE
AESA is automatically available when HWA is active and cannot be disabled separately by the driver.
AESA Operation
AESA uses driver hands-on detection to help determine whether the driver is available to operate the vehicle. If hands-off driving is detected for more than 40 seconds, AESA may start a minimum risk maneuver.
AESA helps keep the vehicle within the lane while slowing to a stop. The system does not change lanes to the road shoulder.
When AESA is decelerating the vehicle:
The hazard lights turn on
AESA helps keep the vehicle within the lane
AESA controls braking to slow the vehicle to a stop
After vehicle comes to complete stop:
eCall will be triggered
EPB will be applied
Doors will be unlocked
AESA will be off
The minimum risk maneuver is intended to bring the vehicle to a standstill unless the system is deactivated by the driver during the maneuver.
WARNING AESA is designed for emergencies when the driver is not responding. Do not rely on AESA as a stopping method under normal circumstances.
Always keep your hands on the steering wheel and remain prepared to take full control of the vehicle. Failure to remain alert and in control may result in property damage, serious injury, or death.
After AESA brings the vehicle to a stop in lane, doors will unlock automatically and an eCall will be triggered. While decelerating, deceleration shall be canceled if driver attention is detected by Brake press or hand(s) ON.​

## Lane Assistance

Lane Assist Systems
Overview
Lane Assist systems are a group of Advanced Driver Assistance System which assist the driver in avoiding unintended lane departures and preventing potential collisions via steering intervention. Lane Assist consists of the following features:
Lane Departure Warning (LDW)
Lane Keep Assist (LKA)
Emergency Lane Keeping (ELK)
Lane Departure Warning (LDW)
Lane Departure Warning (LDW) is a driver assistance system that warns drivers when their vehicle unintentionally drifts towards the lane markers by using a visual warning icon, a warning sound and haptic steering wheel feedback by vibrating the steering wheel. This feature does not actively apply steering intervention.
LDW will be active whenever any Lane Assist mode is enabled.
Lane Keep Assist (LKA)
Lane Keep Assist (LKA) is a driver assistance system that actively supports the driver in maintaining the vehicle within its current lane. When the system detects the vehicle unintentionally drifting towards either lane marker or road edge it adjusts the vehicle path by corrective steering.
LKA is available when Lane Assist (LA) is turned on and Auto-steer is enabled.
Emergency Lane Keeping (ELK)
Emergency Lane Keeping (ELK) is a driver assistance system that avoids or lessens the severity of accidents by automatically applying a steering correction when a vehicle is about to drift beyond a solid lane marking, the edge of the road or into oncoming or overtaking traffic in an adjacent lane.
ELK recognizes lane markings, road edges, and vehicles traveling in the same or opposite direction in the adjacent lane and controls the steering wheel to prevent the vehicle from drifting or colliding with other vehicles in the adjacent lane.
ELK will be active whenever  Emergency Lane Keeping  Mode is enabled.
ELK Oncoming scenario shall work only on driver side when Turn indicator is OFF.
ELK overtaking scenario shall not be suppressed by Turn Indicator signals.
How Lane Assist Works
The Lane Keeping assist system works by using a camera mounted on the windshield that collects information about traffic conditions ahead including:
Types of lane markings.
Distance from the vehicle to road markings or road edges.
Curvature of the road.
Proximity to nearby vehicles.
Based on these inputs, Lane Assist will alert or actively provide steering intervention to correct the vehicle path.
Lane Assist Operation
Lane Assist has several modes of operation that can be selected in the Driver Assistance Page. Depending on which mode is selected one or more of the Lane Assist features will be activated.
The system will be automatically activated when the vehicle speed is between 60 km/h - 150 km/h.
For ELK Overtaking scenario the system will be automatically activated when the vehicle speed is between approximately 60 - 150 km/h.
* Maximum Limit as mentioned, is the feature of system, customer is liable to follow laws of the land as applicable while driving.
To access Lane Assist settings:
Tap  ADAS    in the Driver View area on the screen.
O r, Tap  App Library    >  Drive Aids  .
Tap  Driving Assistance    >  Lane.
Toggle Lane Assist (LA)  ON/OFF.  If Lane Assist (LA) is ON, you can choose from the options below:
Tap the toggle button to turn  ON/OFF  Auto-steer.
Tap on the Chime toggle to Mute/Unmute audio chime (audible warning).
Tap on the Haptics for alerts toggle to turn On/Off haptic feedback through steering wheel vibration (tactile alert).
Select the desired sensitivity level - Low/Medium/High.
NOTE
Lane Assist will default ON at every ignition cycle depending on the Market or User Profile selection. Please contact VinFast for more information on applicability for the Market.
To access Emergency Lane Keeping settings:
Tap  ADAS    in the Driver View area on the screen.
O r, Tap  App Library    >  Drive Aids  .
Tap  Driving Assistance    >  Lane.
Toggle Emergency Lane Keeping (ELK)  ON/OFF .
Tap on the Haptics for alerts toggle to turn On/Off haptic feedback through steering wheel vibration (tactile alert).
Lane Assist Displays and Warnings:
Depending on readiness and the presence and type of lane markings in front of the vehicle the Lane Assist system will project various indicators and alerts on the Infotainment Display.
The presence and color of lane markings for both the left and right side of the vehicle be displayed accordingly:
No Lane Detected - No lane lines are displayed
Lane(s) Detected - Lane Keeping Assist (LKA) and Emergency Lane Keeping (ELK) settings are disabled: Display lane markings only.
Lane(s) Detected - Lane Keeping Assist (LKA) or Emergency Lane Keeping (ELK) settings are enabled: Display a Black/White icon
Lane(s) Detected - Warning/Alert/Intervention:
Lane Departure Warning (LDW) Active: A yellow line, an alert sound and vibration feedback will appear throughout the event.
Lane Keeping Assist (LKA) Active: A single corrective steering adjustment with a mild intervention level will occur. This is indicated by a yellow icon  .
Emergency Lane Keeping Assist (ELK) Active: A notification message will be displayed on the central control screen along with a yellow icon  .
How to Deactivate Lane Assist:
The Lane Assist system is deactivated by any of the following conditions:
The speed of the vehicle is less than approximately 60 km/h.
The speed of the vehicle is over approximately 150 km/h.
* Maximum Limit as mentioned, is the feature of system, customer is liable to follow laws of the land as applicable while driving.
The driver uses the turn indicator while changing lanes.
Lost detection of lane markings.
Another active safety system intervenes (Anti-lock Braking System (ABS), Electronic Stability Control (ESC).
The quality of the detected two-lane lines is not high.
Driver override (apply high steering wheel rotation and steering override torque).
If Automatic Emergency Braking (AEB) is activated.
If Highway Assist (HWA) is activated, ELK is disabled.
Tyre pressure too low (Tyre alarm detected).
Traction Control System (TCS) is not available.
Trunk door or Bonnet door is Opened.
If Hazard lights are ON.
Turn of the Lane Assist.
When connect with trailer.
Vehicle is not running in forward (Gear shift position is not in D)
NOTE
A malfunction warning will be triggered when a failure is detected in the Lane Assist system and will be shown on the Infotainment screen to notify the driver.
WARNING
Do not rely completely on the Lane Assist System to keep the vehicle within its lane or avoid collisions with other vehicles.
Always pay attention to the road and maintain a proper vehicle position within the lane otherwise vehicle damage, injury, or death could occur.
The functions of Lane Assist have limitations. Always stay on course using the steering wheel and drive with care.
The Lane Assist system does not continuously steer the vehicle.
Lane assist system may not detect the lane marking, road edge properly because camera and radar is blocked or occluded by dirt, snow, or glared by high exposure light sources.
Lane Assist system may not detect lane markings in poor visibility conditions (rain, fog, snow, faded markings, etc.). The driver must always remain attentive and maintain safe control of the vehicle, without relying solely on the system.
It is recommended to turn the system OFF when driving on slippery roads. Otherwise, this could cause loss of control of the vehicle which can cause accidents.
Do not use or activate Lane Assist Systems when towing anything such as trailer, recreational vehicles, etc. Failure to comply may result in property damage, serious injury or death.
NOTE
Limitation of lane assist systems. Lane Assist may not operate properly or may operate unexpectedly under the following circumstances:
• The lane markings (or road edges) are complicated or a structure substitutes for the lines, such as a construction area etc.
• The lane suddenly disappears, such as at a junction etc.
• There is a boundary structure in the roadway, such as a tollgate, sidewalk, kerb, etc.
• The color of the lane marking (or road edge) is not distinguishable from the road.
• There are markings (or road edges) on the road looks similar like lanes (example faded lanes/ Road joints etc.)
• The lane marking (or road edge) is indistinct or damaged.
• The lane (or road width) is very wide or narrow.
• It is the driver’s responsibility to safely drive and control the vehicle. Do not solely rely on LKA/ELK and drive dangerously.
• If the vehicle is driven at high speed on curve road, the steering wheel may not be controlled. Driver should always be ready to take the control.
• When any Abnormality in steering control, observe function status in MHU/HUD and take control if necessary.

## Collision Avoidance

F-AEB - Front Automatic Emergency Brake
Front Automatic Emergency Braking (F-AEB) is designed to reduce the risk of frontal collisions. In the event that the system detects a delay or absence of deliberate reaction of the driver to an impending collision, it may automatically apply a strong brake to reduce the severity of the collision. F-AEB can detect cars, and vulnerable road users (pedestrians) (*)
Drivers must not rely solely on the performance of the F-AEB feature as it may malfunction or not perform as expected.
Drivers are responsible for remaining vigilant and in control of the vehicle at all times. Failure to do so may lead to damage, serious injury, harm or death. In situations where both the driver and F-AEB initiate braking deceleration, the maximum braking deceleration from both the driver and F-AEB will be selected and applied.
NOTE
When F-AEB is turned OFF a telltale will be shown in the Car Control Area to inform the driver.
When a new User Profile is created, F-AEB shall be initialized to OFF. Any subsequent user modification of the F-AEB setting shall be stored within that User Profile and restored after each ignition cycle until changed by the user.
F-AEB may reduce or cancel automatic emergency braking during an intervention when:
• The driver deliberately presses the accelerator pedal to override the braking intervention or makes a rapid steering input to change the vehicle’s direction.
• The system determines that the risk of collision is no longer imminent.
F-AEB operates efficiently​ when the vehicle travels​ at the speed between 10 km/h to 60 km/h.
F-AEB will not be activated for crossing cars, oncoming cars, cars traveling on the same direction at speed above 25 kph(**)​
F-AEB performance may be reduced, or the system may not operate as intended, in the following situations (**) :​
• In complex traffic or highly dynamic surroundings where vehicles, pedestrians, or other objects cannot be reliably detected or classified.​
• When pedestrians, vehicles, or other road users suddenly or rapidly move into the vehicle’s path.​
• When road users are close to, partially obscured by, or hidden behind other vehicles or objects.​
• When a pedestrian, vehicle, or other road user cannot be clearly distinguished from the background.​
• When driving through tight bends or during dynamic or aggressive driving.​
• When encountering large vehicles, such as trucks or buses, or vehicles with unusual shapes or configurations.​
• In complex driving situations, F-AEB may issue a warning or apply the brakes even when there is no immediate risk of collision. Always maintain a safe speed and sufficient distance from vehicles, pedestrians, and other road users. Do not rely solely on F-AEB to prevent a collision.​
• F-AEB braking intervention may be reduced or cancelled when the driver presses the brake pedal.​
• For safety reasons, F-AEB cannot be switched off using the On/Off control while the vehicle speed is above 10 km/h.
To enable/disable F-AEB:
Tap  ADAS    in the Driver View area on the screen.   Or, tap  App Library    >  Drive Aids  .
Tap  Collision Avoidance    .
Scroll down to find  Automatic Emergency Braking (AEB) .
Turn  Front  ON/OFF.
WARNING
Drivers must not rely solely on the performance of the F-AEB feature as it may malfunction or not always perform as expected.
Performance may vary based on road conditions or other factors. Even when it performs as expected, the feature may only reduce the severity of a collision.
Drivers are responsible for remaining vigilant and in control of the vehicle at all times. Failure to do so may lead to damage, serious injury, harm or death.
Features performance may be degraded or inoperative under varying conditions, including rain, snowfall, glare, dust, fog, condensation, low light, low visibility due to system limitations. F-AEB is inoperative at nighttime condition (**). Drivers must remain attentive and maintain full control of the vehicle at all times.​
F-AEB may not detect an impending collision and apply the brake if another vehicle or object is occluded, partially in its path, or if debris is present on the road. It is the driver’s responsibility to drive safely and remain in control of the vehicle at all times.
F-AEB does not avoid or mitigate collisions from the side of the vehicle, always pay attention while driving.
Do not use F-AEB as a substitute for foot braking.
Do not use or activate this feature when towing anything such as a trailer or recreational vehicle. Failure to comply may result in property damage, serious injury, or death.
(*) The object types detected by F-AEB may be extended through a future software update.
(**) Performance in these situations may be enhanced through future software updates.

## Collision Warning Systems

Forward Collision Warning
Forward Collision Warning (FCW) alerts the driver if an imminent frontal collision is detected with objects such as cars, motorcyclists, and vulnerable road users such as pedestrians, bicyclists (*)​. This feature does not actively intervene with braking or steering input. FCW takes information from radar and/or cameras to calculate the distance of objects from the vehicle in real time.
Forward Collision Warning Stages
FCW may be shown in the Driver View area in the following stages:
No warning - Preconditions are not satisfied or activation is not allowed.
Distance warning - A visual warning is shown.
Forward collision warning -  FCW icon  , and a warning prompt is shown, and a repeated chime sounds.
NOTE
If Automatic Emergency Braking (AEB) is ON, Forward Collision Warning (FCW) will automatically be turned on to warn the driver before any braking.​
Forward Collision Warning will default ON after each ignition cycle, depending on Market or User Profile selection. Please contact VinFast for more information on its applicability to your market.
FCW can operate effectively when the vehicle is traveling at the speed between 10 kph and 60 kph​
FCW will not be activated for crossing cars, oncoming cars, or cars traveling in the same direction at speed above 25 kph(**)​
FCW will not be activated for crossing motorcyclists traveling at speed above 25 kph(**)
FCW will not be activated for oncoming motorcyclist crossing the vehicle’s path during a turn when the motorcyclist’s speed or the vehicle speed exceeds 25 kph(**)
In some situations, the ability of FCW to detect objects may be limited:
• Objects located above the coverage area of the sensors (radars and/or cameras)​, such as garage doors that are partially open, or objects that are hanging.
• Low or occluded obstacle.
• Narrow objects, such as barrier chains, poles, or fences.
• Projecting objects, such as trailer draw bars.
• Objects with certain surfaces and structures, such as fabric.
To enable/disable​ the FCW:
Tap  ADAS    in the Driver View area on the screen.
O r, Tap  App Library    >  Drive Aids  .
Tap  Collision Avoidance    .
Toggle Forward Collision Warning (FCW)  ON/OFF .
Change the system sensitivity via the toggle button - Low/Medium/High.
NOTE
Detection and warning performance depend on the target object type, driving scenario, and vehicle speed.
FCW cannot be turned off using the On/Off toggle button when the vehicle speed exceeds 10 km/h
CAUTION
FCW can issue unnecessary warnings when its function is impaired, e.g., if the sensors (radars and/or cameras)​ are dirty or their positions have been changed. Always make sure that all the sensors (radars and/or cameras)​ in the vehicle are clean before driving.
Do not stick objects, such as stickers, films, or protectors, near or in front of radars and cameras.
WARNING The area being monitored by FCW can be adversely affected by road and weather conditions so always drive with caution.
FCW is designed only to provide visual and audible alerts. The system does not apply brakes or slow down the vehicle to avoid a collision. When seeing and/or hearing a warning, it is the driver’s responsibility to take immediate corrective action.
FCW may provide a warning in situations where the likelihood of collision may not exist. Do not depend completely on the system and always pay attention while driving to avoid any type of collision.
(*) The object types detected by FCW may be extended through a future software update.
(**) Performance in these conditions may be enhanced through a future software update.
Blind Spot Detection
Blind Spot Detection (BSD) is a driver assistance system that monitors the adjacent lanes next to the vehicle and warns if an object is present in the driver’s blind spot. It will warn for objects the driver cannot see and warn for objects that approach fast from behind. This feature does not actively intervene or provide steering input.
Using radar, Turn indicator and vehicle speed BSD detects objects in the blind spot and calculates the distance of these objects from the vehicle.
When a moving object is detected in the vehicle’s blind spot, the LED blind spot detection lamp   will illuminate on the corresponding side mirror. In addition, if the driver attempts to change lane, the LED blind spot detection lamp   will flash and an audible chime will sound.
The function will automatically activate as soon as vehicle speed is 20 km/h (12.4 mph) or greater.
When the ego vehicle is overtaking a target vehicle, BSD provides a warning when the relative speed is less than 15 km/h (9.3 mph).
To enable/disable BSD:
Tap  ADAS    in the Driver View area on the screen.
O r, Tap  App Library    >  Drive Aids  .
Tap  Collision Avoidance    .
Scroll down to find  Blind Spot Detection (BSD) .
Toggle Blind Spot Detection (BSD)  ON/OFF .
Tap on the Chime toggle to Mute/Unmute audio chime (audible warning).
NOTE
Blind Spot Detection and the BSD chime are ON by default at each ignition cycle depending on Market or User Profile selection. Please contact VinFast for more information on applicable for the market.
Conditions under which the BSD may not function correctly​
BSD may not correctly detect vehicles and/or objects in the following conditions:
Bad weather such as heavy rain, fog, snow etc.
Ice or mud etc. attached to the rear bumper.
Driving on a road surface that is wet due to rain, standing water, etc.
There is a significant difference in speed between your vehicle and the vehicle that enters the detection area.
A vehicle is in the detection area from a stop and remains in the detection area as your vehicle accelerates.
Driving up or down consecutive steep inclines, such as hills or a dip in the road, etc.
Multiple vehicles approaching with only a small gap between each vehicle.
Vehicle lanes are wide, and the vehicle in the next lane is too far away from your vehicle.
The vehicle that enters the detection area is traveling at about the same speed as your vehicle.
There is a significant difference in height between your vehicle and the vehicle that enters the detection area.
Towing a trailer (if available).
Vehicles traveling in the opposite direction​.
BSD may unnecessarily detect a vehicle and/or objects under the following conditions:
Short distance between your vehicle and guardrails, walls, signs, parked vehicles and similar stationary objects.
Short distance between your vehicle and a following vehicle.
Vehicle lanes are narrow and a vehicle driving two lanes across from your vehicle enters the detection area.
Items such as a bicycle carrier that are installed on the rear of the vehicle.
Warning timing may vary depending on the vehicle that is approaching.
System Malfunction Warnings
If a fault is detected with the BSD system or an associated ADAS system, it will trigger an alert message on the Infotainment screen to alert the driver. If a malfunction alert is displayed, some or all of the system functionality may be impacted.
WARNING The driver is fully responsible for changing lanes carefully and for checking if it is safe to do so.
Drivers must not solely rely on BSD as the feature may malfunction and may not always perform as expected.
The system may not provide alerts under all driving conditions, including when driving around tight curves.
The system also does not alert drivers to vehicles outside of the detection zones.
Performance may also vary based on other conditions or factors.
Drivers are responsible for remaining vigilant and in control of the vehicle at all times. Failure to do so may lead to damage, serious injury, harm or death.
CAUTION
The detection range may vary depending on the speed of the vehicle. Even if there is a vehicle in a blind spot zone, BSD may not activate a warning when passing at high speed.
The BSD sensors may become impaired by mud, rain, frost, ice or other factor. Performance may be subsequently affected and not alert the driver to vehicles in the lane on either side. Always keep sensors clean and remain vigilant at all times.
Warning timing may vary depending on the speed of the vehicle that is approaching.
Rear Cross Traffic Alert
Rear Cross Traffic Alert (RCTA) is a driver assistance system that warns drivers of an imminent collision from object crossing on the reverse trajectory of rear vehicle of the vehicle while reversing. This feature does not actively intervene via steering input or braking but provides both audible and visual alerts.
RCTA uses radar sensors in the rear corners of the vehicle to detect moving objects that are approaching including the following:
Vehicles
Motorcycles
Bicycles
CAUTION
The RCTA function will not generate warnings for stationary objects or objects that are not road users, e.g. light poles, garbage containers etc.
Pedestrians may or may not be detected based on size and/or clothing.
RCTA Operation
RCTA Visual and Audible Alerts
Depending on the situation, the RCTA warning will appear on the left, right, or both sides of the rear camera viewport.
The LED blind spot detection lamp   will illuminate on the corresponding side mirror closest to the oncoming object. In addition, if the collision risk increases, the LED blind spot detection lamp   will flash and an audible chime will sound.
RCTA Activation
The function is available when the following conditions are met:
The gear mode is in Reverse (R) or Neutral (N).
Ego vehicle speed is 18 km/h (11.2 mph) or less.
The RCTA system is turned on.
NOTE
Rear Cross Traffic Alert is ON by default at each ignition cycle.
To enable/disable RCTA:
Tap  ADAS    in the Driver View area on the screen.
O r, Tap  App Library    >  Drive Aids  .
Tap  Collision Avoidance    .
Scroll down to find  Cross Traffic Alert (CTA) .
Turn  Rear  ON/OFF.
Conditions under which the RCTA may not function correctly​​
RCTA may not detect correctly vehicles and/or objects in the following conditions:
Bad weather such as heavy rain, fog, snow etc.
Ice or mud etc. attached to the rear bumper.
Driving on a road surface that is wet due to rain, standing water, etc.
There is a significant difference in speed between your vehicle and the vehicle that enters the detection area.
A vehicle is in the detection area from a stop and remains in the detection area as your vehicle accelerates.
Driving up or down consecutive steep inclines, such as hills or a dip in the road, etc.
Multiple vehicles approaching with only a small gap between each vehicle.
Vehicle lanes are wide, and the vehicle in the next lane is too far away from your vehicle.
The vehicle that enters the detection area is traveling at about the same speed as your vehicle.
There is a significant difference in height between your vehicle and the vehicle that enters the detection area.
Directly after the BSD main switch is set to ON.
Towing a trailer (if avaliable).
Vehicles traveling in the opposite direction​.
RCTA may unnecessarily detect a vehicle and/or objects under the following conditions:
Short distance between your vehicle and guardrails, walls, signs, parked vehicles and similar stationary objects.
Short distance between your vehicle and a following vehicle.
Vehicle lanes are narrow and a vehicle driving two lanes across from your vehicle enters the detection area.
Items such as a bicycle carrier that are installed on the rear of the vehicle.
Warning timing may vary depending on the vehicle that is approaching.
System Faults:
In the event of a RCTA fault, a warning will be displayed on the Infotainment screen. Vehicle alert details can be viewed in the Service app.
See >   Services > Vehicle Status  .
WARNING Do not rely completely on RCTA to detect a potential collision, always check the surrounding area before reversing.
RCTA warning timing may vary depending on the speed of approaching vehicle.
Use the side mirrors and check surroundings for vehicles, motorcycles, bicycles or pedestrians that may be approaching the vehicle from the rear.
The system may not react if objects are approaching rapidly.
RCTA will not provide alerts if your vehicle is parallel parked or if your vehicle is pulled too far into the parking space so that it is hidden by adjacent vehicles.
The RCTA chime may not be heard if the surrounding area is noisy.
CAUTION
The sensors may become impaired by mud, rain, frost or ice and fail to detect approaching objects. Always ensure all sensors and cameras in the vehicle are clean prior to driving.
Door Opening Warning
Door Opening Warning (DOW) is a driver assistance system that warns occupants inside a stationary vehicle when other road users are approaching the vehicle from the rear and collision risk is high. The system assists occupants to safely exit a vehicle and prevent serious accidents associated with vehicle doors opening in front of oncoming traffic.
DOW uses radars in the rear bumpers to detect oncoming traffic. If an oncoming vehicle is detected, occupants will be alerted via LEDs on the side mirrors. ﻿Additionally, ambient lighting (if available) will be used as a warning mechanism in combination with directional audible warnings for all occupants inside the vehicle.
NOTE
The DOW Function will not generate warnings for stationary target objects and elements.
The DOW alert will persist as long as the target object fulfills the warning conditions.
Door Opening Warning Operation
When Door Opening Warning (DOW) is active the following will occur:
An   icon will be shown on the corresponding door side view mirror.
If the impacted door is opened, this icon will start flashing and a chime will be heard.
If the corresponding door is closed and an oncoming object is still detected, then the mirror icon will remain and the flashing and chime will cease.
Depending on the situation, the DOW warning will also appear on the left, right, or both sides of the vehicle icon on the screen.
Door Opening Warning (DOW) activates:
When vehicle speed is less than 1.8 km/h.
The approaching vehicle speed is more than 6.84 km/h
Door Opening Warning (DOW) deactivates:
When vehicle speed is greater than 1.8 km/h.
The approaching vehicle speed is less than 6.84 km/h
To enable/disable Door Opening Warning (DOW) :
Tap  ADAS    in the Driver View area on the screen.   OR tap  App Library    >  Drive Aids  .
Tap  Collision Avoidance    .
Scroll down to find  Door Opening Warning (DOW) .
Turn this feature ON/OFF.
Tap on the Auto-lock door toggle to enable or disable the automatic door locking function.
NOTE
After each ignition cycle, the Door Opening Warning system will default to Warning Only mode.
The feature works before and shortly after ignition OFF, around 30 minutes.
Conditions under which the DOW may not function correctly​​
DOW may not detect correctly vehicles and/or objects in the following conditions:
Bad weather such as heavy rain, fog, snow etc.
Ice or mud etc. attached to the rear bumper.
Driving on a road surface that is wet due to rain, standing water, etc.
There is a significant difference in speed between your vehicle and the vehicle that enters the detection area.
A vehicle is in the detection area from a stop and remains in the detection area as your vehicle accelerates.
Driving up or down consecutive steep inclines, such as hills or a dip in the road, etc.
Multiple vehicles approaching with only a small gap between each vehicle.
Vehicle lanes are wide, and the vehicle in the next lane is too far away from your vehicle.
The vehicle that enters the detection area is traveling at about the same speed as your vehicle.
There is a significant difference in height between your vehicle and the vehicle that enters the detection area.
Directly after the BSD main switch is set to ON.
Towing a trailer (if available).
Vehicles traveling in the opposite direction​.
DOW may unnecessarily detect a vehicle and/or objects under the following conditions:
Short distance between your vehicle and guardrails, walls, signs, parked vehicles and similar stationary objects.
Short distance between your vehicle and a following vehicle.
Vehicle lanes are narrow and a vehicle driving two lanes across from your vehicle enters the detection area.
Items such as a bicycle carrier that are installed on the rear of the vehicle.
Warning timing may vary depending on the vehicle that is approaching.
System Faults
A malfunction warning will be triggered when a failure is detected in the DOW system or with other related ADAS systems and will be shown on the Infotainment screen to notify the driver.
WARNING Opening a door when an object is approaching may cause damage or personal injury. Always use the side mirrors and complete a head check to watch for vehicles, motorcycles, bicycles or pedestrians that may be approaching the vehicle.
Door Opening Warning is a driver assistance function only. It does not replace the driver’s or occupants’ responsibility to check for approaching traffic before opening a door.
CAUTION
The rear sensors may become blocked by mud, rain, frost or ice and fail to detect approaching objects. Always ensure sensors are clean before starting driving.

## Park Assist

Front & Rear Parking Assist
The Front & Rear Parking Assist system assists the driver when parking by alerting them to surrounding objects. The system provides audible alerts to inform and warn the driver. Park Assist (PA) sensors are located on both the front and rear bumpers to identify surrounding obstacles.
CAUTION
Do not push, scratch or strike a sensor as this may cause damage.
Do not spray the sensor or its surrounding area directly with high pressure water.
NOTE
Ensure all park assist sensors are free from the build up of snow, ice, dirt or mud which may impact their operation. For details on cleaning the sensors. See >   Vehicle Care > Radar, Sensor, and Camera Care  .
Front Parking Assist (if equipped)
Front Parking Assist (FPA) is a driver assistance system that warns the driver of nearby objects (including the sidewalk) in front of the vehicle when parking.
Rear Parking Assist
Rear Parking Assist (RPA) is a driver assistance system that warns the driver of nearby objects (including the sidewalk) behind the vehicle when parking.
Parking Assist status
When the system detects an object, the related area on the screen will be highlighted. As the vehicle gets closer to the object, the color of this area will change from yellow to red. At the same time, the system will sound a warning. As the distance to the object decreases, the beeps will sound faster. When the vehicle is very close to the object, the beeps may become a continuous tone.
CAUTION
The Front & Rear Parking Assist is not a safety system, and its design intention is to provide assistance during parking. Even if used, the system cannot replace your attention and judgment.
NOTE
The Parking assist chime  shall be automatically muted when car is at very low speed or stationary
To activate Front & Rear Parking Assist:
Front Parking Assist is automatically activated when vehicle’s speed is less than 10 km/h and the gear is in Drive (D).
Rear Parking Assist is automatically activated when the gear is in Reverse (R) or when the car is rolling back.
NOTE
When Car Wash Mode is active, the parking assist system will be temporarily disabled without any warnings. The system will restore automatically when Car Wash Mode is exited.
To turn ON/OFF Parking Assist chime:
Tap Mute icon when available on Driver View Area.
NOTE
Parking Assist chime Mute/Unmute button only displays when Parking Assist is activated.
Park Assist Faults
If a fault is detected, the parking assist system will display a warning message on the infotainment screen, and the function may be degraded or disabled as needed.
If a Park Assist sensor has a fault due to dirt or a blockage, clean the impacted sensor.
There are situations in which the Parking Assist system might operate unexpectedly:
Uneven road condition, gravel roads, rails, repairing road or fallen leaves on the road.
Driving in incline/decline conditions.
Driving in the flooded road.
After sales toolkit such as bumper protector etc. is installed in the bumpers.
Driving in bad weather such as heavy rain, fog, snow or a sandstorm.
Driving in the heavy traffic jam condition.
Driving in strong wind are blowing, or beside a big car (truck, bus etc).
The orientation ultrasonic sensor has been changed due to collision or other impacts.
WARNING
The driver must always remain alert and in full control of the vehicle at all times including during parking maneuvers.
Park Assist may not detect potholes, trenches or drains.
Under certain conditions, ultrasonic waves may be absorbed or deflected by soft surfaces, angled objects, clothing, or external noise sources, causing the system to miss or inaccurately detect obstacles. Extra caution is required with low-height obstacles that may not be reliably detected and could damage the lower bumper.
Always check the surrounding area when reversing and using the Rear Parking Assist System.
It is not recommended to use or activate Rear Parking Assist when towing anything such as trailer, recreational vehicles, etc. Failure to comply may result in property damage, serious injury or death.
Rear View Camera
The Rear View Camera is used to display the area behind the vehicle and to assist with reversing or parking. The system uses a camera mounted above the rear license plate and displays an image on the Infotainment screen.
How to Activate the Rear View Camera
The Rear View Camera is automatically activated when reverse gear is selected.
The Rear View Camera can also be activated manually by tapping the Camera   icon in App View on the infotainment screen.
When Reverse (R) gear is selected, the parking assist is also activated and provides guidelines and audio feedback during parking. See >   Front & Rear Parking Assist  .
The dynamic guidelines show the driving path relative to the steering wheel position.
How to Deactivate the Rear View Camera
The Rear View Camera is automatically deactivated if the driver disengages Reverse (R) gear or when vehicle speed is higher than or equal to 25 km/h in Drive (D) gear (Can also manually deactivate the camera by tapping the  X  button on the infotainment screen).
NOTE
The Rear View Camera cannot be deactivated while the vehicle is in Reverse (R) gear.
WARNING
Keep the Rear View Camera lenses clear, as unclear lenses will impact vision and objects may not be detected leading to a collision.
Always check vehicle surroundings while using the Rear View Camera as camera(s) may not always detect objects or barriers that can potentially cause damage or injury.
The camera(s) may not display children, pedestrians, cyclists, traffic, animals, or any other objects outside of the camera’s field of view.
The distance of images displayed may vary from actual.
Do not obstruct the Rear View Camera by installing objects, such as a bumper sticker or a bumper guard, near the Rear View Camera.
360 Surround View Monitoring
The 360 Surround View Monitoring System uses multiple cameras along with graphical markings to assist the driver in low-speed parking maneuvers.
The system works in parallel with the Park Assist System and provides multiple viewing options from cameras mounted in the Side Mirrors, under Hood, and Liftgate. Depending on the display mode, some of these views are combined to provide a complete 360-degree view around the vehicle. ﻿Camera locations are shown below:
NOTE
To ensure optimum system performance, please ensure each camera is free from dirt or obstruction.
Performance of the Surround View Monitor may be reduced in heavy fog conditions.
WARNING
The driver has full responsibility for vehicle operation while using the Surround View Monitoring System.
Areas near the corners of the vehicle may be obscured from camera view, resulting in some surrounding objects not being detected.
Do not fold in the outside mirrors of the vehicle when using Surround View Monitoring. Doing so will limit the effectiveness of the vehicle’s side cameras and display performance.
Objects and obstacles above the cameras are not displayed.
Surround View Monitoring is designed to be used on a flat surface. Roads with different heights such as curbs and speed bumps may look different on the Infotainment screen display.
Always check vehicle surroundings. Failure to comply may result in property damage, serious injury or death.
Surround View Monitor Operation
To activate the Surround View Monitoring System:
Pressing on Camera app   in App Library. This button will be disabled & dimmed when speed condition not met
Shifting to R (always)
To deactivate the Surround View Monitoring System:
The Surround View Monitoring (SVM) will be deactivated under any of the following conditions:
The vehicle is shifted to D (Drive) and the vehicle speed reaches or exceeds 25 km/h.
The Home button is pressed.
The vehicle is shifted to P (Park) or N (Neutral) while the camera was automatically activated in R (Reverse); if the gear is shifted from R to D, the SVM will remain active and switch to the Front View instead of closing.
The X button in the Camera view is pressed.
NOTE
The Surround View Monitoring cannot be deactivated while the vehicle is in Reverse (R) gear.
Camera Display Modes﻿ and Settings
The Surround View Monitoring System offers several camera views and display modes.
