The camera(s) may not display children, pedestrians, cyclists, crossing traffic, animals, or any other object outside of the camera’s field of view.

## Other

AHB - Automatic High Beam
Automatic High Beam (AHB) is a driver assistance feature that supports correct use of the vehicle’s high beam headlights. It automatically turns high beam ON when the road ahead is sufficiently dark and turns high beam OFF when oncoming or preceding vehicles are detected to help prevent glare and inconvenience to other drivers. The system will also automatically turn high beam OFF in areas with sufficient lighting, such as urban areas with adequate street lighting. The AHB system receives information about objects located within the field of view of the front windshield camera.
The following scenarios will cause the AHB system to switch from high beam to low beam:
If oncoming or preceding vehicles are detected.
In urban areas with sufficient streetlights, at junctions with traffic lights or in tunnels.
The AHB system will turn the high beam back ON once the conditions listed above are no longer detected and the operating conditions are met.
NOTE
AHB is activated when the vehicle speed is at or above 40 km/h (25 mph), and is deactivated when the vehicle speed is below 25 km/h (15 mph).
The vehicle headlight control must be set to AUTO to enable the Automatic High Beam feature.
To activate/deactivate the Auto High Beam feature:
Tap App Library   on the touchscreen.
Or, Slide down from top of touchscreen to open Notification Center.
Tap Settings   > Light Settings   > Exterior.
Use the toggle button to turn the feature On/Off.
NOTE
Pushing or pulling the lighting stalk will override the AHB system.
The AHB system uses the front windshield camera to detect oncoming and preceding lights of vehicles, motorcycles, and bicycles.
NOTE
The AHB system may not detect all types of vehicles or road users.
WARNING
Do not use high beam when oncoming traffic is present. Doing so may distract oncoming drivers leading to an accident.
Do not rely completely on the AHB feature. The vehicle may not detect oncoming vehicles in all conditions. Always check for oncoming vehicles and switch to low beam when required.
Automatic headlights are only intended to assist the driver. Full control of vehicle headlights always remains the responsibility of the driver.
NOTE
Do not cover the front windshield camera.
Do not clean the front windshield camera sensor with window cleaner or hazardous solutions, as this may leave a film that can affect sensor operation.
Do not apply any coating on the front windshield, as this may affect AHB headlight performance.
A light sensor is located on top of the windshield. Make sure the sensor is not covered or obstructed, as this may cause headlights to operate when not required.
Use manual operation to turn ON the headlights if required when driving at night in rain, fog, tunnels, or parking facilities.
DMS - Driver Monitoring System (DDAW / ADDW) (if equipped)
Driver Monitoring System (DMS) helps monitor driver alertness and attention while driving. The system can provide Driver Drowsiness Warning (DDAW) if it detects signs of fatigue, and Advanced Driver Distraction Warning (ADDW) if it detects that the driver is not paying sufficient attention to the road. These features are designed to assist the driver and do not replace safe driving practices.
Driver monitoring images are processed inside the vehicle only. The system does not upload camera images or driver monitoring data to the cloud.
The system can detect drowsiness or tiredness in a driver based on a combination of the following:
Eye tracking, such as blink duration, eye movement, and eye closure frequency.
Head tracking, such as head position and head movement.
Facial behavior, such as yawning or squinting.
Vehicle speed.
Time of day.
Lane departure warning frequency.
The system can detect signs of distraction if the driver:
Has been driving for a long time.
Looks away from the road for too long.
Uses a phone while driving.
Does not keep attention on the road ahead.
Takes hands off the steering wheel.
NOTE
The Driver Monitoring System is automatically enabled with vehicle HV power and cannot be manually disabled by the driver.
Certain sunglasses, especially those with dark or infrared-blocking lenses, may reduce the camera’s ability to detect the driver’s eyes and may affect system performance.
Driver Monitoring Settings
To enable/disable Driver Drowsiness Monitoring (DDM):
Tap  ADAS    in the Driver View area on the screen.
O r, Tap  App Library    >  Drive Aids  .
Tap  Driving Assistance    >  Driving.
Toggle Driver Drowsiness Monitoring (DDM)  ON/OFF .
Tap on the Chime toggle to Mute/Unmute audio chime (audible warning).
To enable/disable Driver Attention Monitoring (DAM):
Tap  ADAS    in the Driver View area on the screen.
O r, Tap  App Library    >  Drive Aids  .
Tap  Driving Assistance    >  Driving.
Toggle Driver Attention Monitoring (DAM)  ON/OFF .
Tap on the Chime toggle to Mute/Unmute audio chime (audible warning).
Select the desired sensitivity level - Low/Medium/High.
NOTE
For markets subject to GSR2 (General Safety Regulation) requirements, certain Driver Monitoring System functions may be enabled by default and cannot be completely disabled.
Driver Monitoring System Alert Types
DMS system will generate alerts if it detects that:
The driver has been driving for a long time.
The driver is drowsy.
The driver’s hands are off the steering wheel.
The driver is using a phone while driving.
The driver is not looking at the road.
The alert will be displayed on the infotainment screen together with visual and audio alerts.
WARNING Always drive carefully and do not rely completely on Driver Monitoring System.
Do not drive the vehicle if you feel tired. Always take a break in these situations to avoid the risk of vehicle accident, injury or death.
In some situations, the DMS may not be able to correctly identify the driver’s state.
DMS performance may be reduced if the driver monitoring camera or infrared light emitters are blocked, if the driver is wearing sunglasses, if the driver’s eyes are hidden behind eyeglass frames, or if the driver is wearing a mask that prevents the system from correctly identifying the driver’s state.

# Driving


## Starting & Shutting Down Vehicle

Starting the Vehicle
Carrying the correct remote key is necessary to unlock and start the vehicle.
To Start Driving the Vehicle:
Unlock and open the driver’s door, and make sure the vehicle has detected the remote key inside.
Press the brake pedal  , “READY” will be displayed on the screen indicating the vehicle is ON.
Ensure all doors, tailgate and front hood are closed, toggle the gear shift lever   up or down to Reverse (R)/Drive (D), and at this time, the vehicle’s EPB function will automatically be released.
Press accelerator pedal   and the vehicle begins to move.
CAUTION
The above operation must be followed to start the car normally. When it cannot start normally, check if the above operation is correct.
Before starting the vehicle, please check and confirm that the seats, steering wheel, and exterior mirrors are adjusted to a safe and comfortable position, and that the brake pedal can be pressed normally.
To Power OFF the Vehicle:
Press the brake pedal   until the vehicle has come to a complete stop.
Press the Park (P)   button.
Ensure all doors, tailgate and front hood are closed, lock the vehicle after leaving with the remote key, the vehicle will automatically power OFF﻿.
WARNING
Do not shift gears while the vehicle is in motion.
Do not accelerate while engaging a gear.
Do not press the accelerator pedal and brake pedal at the same time.
Ensure that all occupants fasten their seatbelt before driving the vehicle.
NOTE
The cooling fan and pump may be heard and remain operating for a period of time after the vehicle has stopped and been locked. This is normal vehicle operation and could be result of:
• The vehicle High Voltage Battery is charging the low voltage (12V) battery.
• The vehicle is in the process of cooling the High Voltage electric motor.
• The vehicle is in the process of a firmware over the air (FOTA) software update.
If this noise continues for an extended period of time, contact the nearest VinFast Service to have the vehicle inspected.
Vehicle Power Mode Information
The vehicle consists of three power modes:
OFF - vehicle is powered OFF and no accessories or vehicle drive gears are available.
Accessory (ACC) - Unlock the vehicle and open the doors, and the vehicle enters ACC mode. At this time, the accessory power mode is activated, allowing access to the infotainment functions on the screen.
ON (Ready) - The vehicle has detected the remote key and pressed the brake pedal, and the vehicle is in P/N gear. The accessory and vehicle drive power are both turned on.
Low Key Fob Battery
If the remote Key Fob battery is weak, the vehicle can still be started.
To Start the Vehicle:
Place the key in the Console Armrest storage area as shown.
Press the brake pedal and select Drive (D) or Reverse (R) gear.
CAUTION
If the vehicle still fails to start after replacing the key fob battery, please contact the VinFast Service Center.

## Drive Systems

Transmission Gear Selector
The vehicle Transmission Gear selector is used to engage different vehicle gear positions, which include Park (P), Reverse (R), Neutral (N) and Drive (D).
The Transmission Gear Selector is located on the right side of the steering wheel.
Park (P): Press the button on the side of the lever to for Park gear.
Drive (D): Push the gear lever down to the maximum position for Drive gear.
Reverse (R): Push the gear lever up to the maximum position for Reverse gear.
Neutral (N): Shift the gear lever to center for Neutral (N) gear.
To select a gear position:
Depress the brake pedal and ensure the vehicle is stationary or has come to a complete stop.
Move the lever to select the desired gear.
The selected gear will be displayed on the screen.
WARNING
Do not speed up while engaging a gear.
Do not press the accelerator pedal and brake pedal at the same time.
Never leave the vehicle unattended while the power is ON.
It is prohibited to use N gear for vehicle coasting while driving.
CAUTION
When the gear is in N gear, the brake pedal should be pressed to prevent slipping.
NOTE
When the driver door is open and Auto Gear P mode is active, the gear lever cannot be shifted into Drive (D) or Reverse (R).
Neutral
In Neutral (N), your vehicle rolls freely when no brakes are applied. Neutral (N) can be used to allow the vehicle to be maneuvered in a workshop environment or to be moved onto a towing truck.
CAUTION
Shifting to Neutral (N) will disable regenerative braking resulting in greater than normal brake pressure being required.
Shifting to Neutral (N) should only be done while a driver is present to apply the vehicle brakes when necessary.
Drive Modes
The vehicle is equipped with multiple driving modes, which can be selected to change the acceleration response and power transmission of the vehicle. The driving mode can be changed through the Infotainment screen.
Available Driving Modes are:
ECO
NORMAL
SPORT
To select a Driving Mode:
Tap  Drive Mode    on the Driver View Area to access the Driving Preferences interface.
Tap to select the desired drive mode.
NOTE
Reasonable use of driving modes can improve power/range economy.
Creep Mode
Creep mode is suitable for driving at lower speeds in stop and go traffic or parking lots. After activating this function, the vehicle will move slowly in the selected direction of travel without the need to press the accelerator pedal.
To activate/deactivate creep mode:
Tap  Drive Mode    on the Driver View Area to access the Driving Preferences interface.
Scroll down to find  Creep Mode .
Toggle creep mode ON/OFF.
WARNING
Do not rely on creep mode to prevent your vehicle from rolling down a hill. Keep your foot firmly on the brake pedal if you wish to remain stationary or on the accelerator pedal if you wish to ascend a hill. Failure to do so may result in accident causing serious injury or death.
Auto Gear P
When the function is activated, it allows the system to automatically switch from Drive (D) or Reverse (R) to Park Mode:
When the vehicle has fully stopped and the driver door is opened, and both the brake pedal and the accelerator pedal are released.
To activate/deactivate Auto Gear P:
Tap  Drive Mode    on the Driver View Area to access the Driving Preferences interface.
Scroll down to find  Auto Gear P .
Toggle Auto Gear P ON/OFF.
WARNING
Always ensure that the vehicle is securely parked before exiting.

## Steering Wheel

Adjustment
Prior to operating the vehicle, it is important to adjust the steering wheel to achieve a comfortable driving position. For additional information regarding a correct seating position, see > Seats >   Correct & Incorrect Seating Positions  .
For an ideal steering position:
The driver always ensures the safest and most comfortable distance from the steering wheel.
The driver should have a clear view of the area around the vehicle.
The driver should always hold the steering wheel with both hands on the outer edges (9 o’clock and 3 o’clock positions) while driving.
Adjustable Steering Column
The Steering Wheel can be adjusted manually forward/backward   and up/down  , as per requirement.
To adjust the steering position:
Push the lever   down to unlock the steering column.
Adjust the steering wheel to preferred position.
Pull up the lever   to lock the steering column in the adjusted position.
WARNING
Do not make steering wheel adjustments when the vehicle is in motion.
Ensure the steering wheel column is secured in locked position before driving.
Do not control the steering wheel by the horn pad. If the Airbag deploys it may result in injury to the driver’s arms, hands and head.
Do not sit close to the steering wheel when driving. In an event of a collision this may interfere with the deployment of Airbags and may increase the risk of occupant injury.
The steering wheel is equipped with a Hand Off Detection (HOD) sensor. Do not cover it with plastic, carbon fiber, plaster, or any other obstructive materials that may interfere with the sensor’s functionality. Damage caused by improper use or failure to follow these instructions will not be covered under warranty.
Steering Wheel Switches
The steering wheel controls provide quick access to a range of vehicle and infotainment functions, enabling operation of key features without taking your attention away from the road to interact with the screen.

## Mirrors

Side Mirrors
Mirrors mounted on the side of the vehicle provide the driver with visibility behind and to the left and right sides of the vehicle.
Mirror Adjustment
Tap  Settings    >  Vehicle Settings    >  Side Mirrors.  OR, Tap  Mirrors    in the Driver View area on the screen.
Select  Left/Right  side mirror to adjust position.
Use the physical buttons on the steering wheel for adjustment.
When the mirror positions are changed by adjusting the side mirror positions in App View window, tap  Save position , and the mirror position will be stored against the current user profile. The  Restore  button is available when a saved mirror position exists and the current mirror position differs from the saved position. Tapping  Restore  returns the mirrors to the saved position.
Mirror Fold/Unfold
Tap  Settings    >  Vehicle Settings    >  Side Mirrors.  OR, Tap  Mirrors    in the Driver View area on the screen.
Select  Fold both/Unfold both  button to fold/unfold the side mirrors.
Heated Mirrors (if equipped)
The heated side mirror feature reduces the effects of fogging and ice buildup on both the exterior side mirrors ensuring clear visibility. Side mirror heating is activated/deactivated automatically when the Rear Demist feature is turned ON/OFF. See >   Climate Control System > Rear Demist  .
Auto Fold
Automatic folding of vehicle side mirrors when locking the doors.
To turn Auto Fold feature ON/OFF:
Tap  Settings    >  Vehicle Settings    >  Side Mirrors.  OR, Tap  Mirrors    in the Driver View area on the screen.
Toggle Auto Fold. Tap to  Off/On .
Automatic Mirror Tilt in Reverse
The side mirrors can automatically tilt downward to aid in parking when Reverse (R) gear is selected. When Drive (D), Park (P), or Neutral (N) gear is selected, the mirrors will return to their normal set orientation.
To turn Mirror Tilt feature ON/OFF:
Tap  Settings    >  Vehicle Settings    >  Side Mirrors.  OR, Tap  Mirrors    in the Driver View area on the screen.
Tap the toggle button to turn on the feature.
WARNING
Before driving always check that the mirrors are adjusted correctly for your driving position .
Make sure that the blind spot location is clear during parking, changing lanes, turning, or overtaking other vehicles. It may cause accident, injury or even death.
Do not drive with exterior side mirrors folded. Ensure that the mirrors are positioned correctly and that the rear view is not restricted by ice, snow, condensation, or other objects.
Do not place your hand near the base of the mirror during mirror folding operation as your fingers may get caught between the exterior mirror and the mirror base.
NOTE
In the case of an electrical malfunction, the mirrors can be adjusted by gently pressing on the edges of the mirror glass.
Rear View Mirror
Use the rear view mirror to view of the area behind the vehicle through the vehicle’s rear window.
Mirror Adjustment
The rear view mirror position can be adjusted manually by hand. Adjust the rear view mirror when the vehicle is stationary and while seated in the correct driving position for a clear view of the area behind the vehicle.
WARNING
Do not adjust the rear view mirror while driving. Doing so can cause distraction leading to an accident, resulting in death or serious injury.
Do not place objects in the rear seat, cargo area, or behind the rear head restraints which could obstruct vision through the rear window.
CAUTION
Do not spray glass cleaner directly on the mirror as this may damage it. Use a soft towel dampened with water.
NOTE
Before driving, always adjust the rear view mirror for a clear view of the area behind the vehicle.
Automatic Dimming
While driving at night, the Rear View Mirror Automatic Dimming feature reduces the headlight glare of vehicles behind.
NOTE
The Auto Dimming function uses an ambient light sensor at the rear of the electrochromic mirror. Always keep the sensor and other mirror surfaces clean and free from obstructions for proper Auto Dimming operation.
Sun Visor & Vanity Mirror
Adjustable sun visors are provided for the driver and front passenger to block sun glare. Each visor is equipped with an illuminated vanity mirror.
Sun Visor Adjustment
Block the sunlight ahead:
Rotate the sun visor downwards   to a desired angle to block the sunlight ahead.
Block side sunlight:
The sun visor can also rotate to the side to block the sunlight from the side windows.
Rotate the sun visor downwards   to the desired angle.
Detach from the mounting clip and swing outward  .
Illuminated Vanity Mirror
The driver and passenger sun visors are equipped with illuminated vanity mirrors. The mirror light switches on automatically when the mirror cover is lifted   and switches off automatically when the mirror cover is closed.
WARNING
Driving with the sun visor in the down position and the vanity mirror light ON can reduce vision of the road, particularly at night.
Sun visors should always be folded away if they are not being used.
When using the sun visor, do not lean too far forward in front of the airbag. The front airbag could cause serious injury if triggered.

High Beam
High beam headlights can be operated manually or by using the Auto High Beam (AHB) function.
For more information about Automatic High Beam, see >   Advanced Driver Assistance Systems (ADAS) > Other > AHB - Automatic High Beam  .
To manually activate / deactivate high beam:
To manually activate the high beam headlights, push the left multi switch stalk forward. The indicator light   will appear on the screen and the high beams will be switched ON. To return to low beam, pull the multi stalk switch towards you. To flash the high beam headlights, pull the stalk towards you momentarily.
WARNING
Do not use high beam when oncoming traffic is present. Doing so may distract oncoming drivers leading to an accident.
When using the AHB feature, the vehicle may not detect oncoming vehicles in all conditions. Always check for oncoming vehicles and switch to low beam when required.
Automatic headlights are only intended to assist the driver. Full control of vehicle headlights always remains the responsibility of the driver.
NOTE
Do not cover the front windshield camera.
Do not clean the front windshield camera sensor with window cleaner or any hazardous solutions as it may leave a film which could affect sensor operation.
Do not apply any coating on the front windshield as this may impact AHB headlight performance.
A light sensor is located on top of the windshield. Ensure that the sensor is not covered or obstructed as this may result in headlights operating when not required.
Use manual operation to turn ON the headlights if required when driving at night in rain, fog, tunnels and when using parking facilities.
Turn Signals
The vehicle Turn Signals are controlled using the multi switch stalk located on the left-side of the steering wheel.
To operate the Turn Signals:
To flash signal 3 times: Push the multi switch stalk up   or down  lightly, the multi switch stalk will automatically return to its original position
To flash continuously: Push the multi switch stalk up   or down   fully.
The turn signals   and   on the entertainment screen will flash according to the frequency of external turn signals flashing.
To cancel the Turn Signals:
The turn signal will be canceled when:
When the lever is pushed to its maximum position, if the steering wheel rotates back to its original position by a certain angle, the lever will automatically return to the middle position.
When the turn signal is in a continuous flashing state, manually control the lever to return to the middle position.
The 3-flash sequence has ended, if not on continuously.
WARNING
Improper use of lighting can increase the risk of accidents.
NOTE
The Daytime Running Lights (DRL) will turn off while the indicator light is flashing.
Hazard Warning Flashers
The Hazard Warning Flashers warn other drivers to take caution. It should be used in the event of an accident, or if pulled over on the side of the road, or when the vehicle is stopped on the motorway.
To activate/deactivate the Hazard Warning Flashers:
The hazard warning switch is located on the middle area of the front roof.
Press the hazard warning switch, and the hazard warning flashers will start flashing. Press the switch again to turn off the hazard warning flashers.
The signal light     on the infotainment screen flashes when the hazard warning flashers are turned on.
NOTE
The hazard warning flashers can operate even if the vehicle is OFF.
Hazard warning flashers are automatically activated in the event of an emergency braking situation or airbag deployment.

Stop/Brake Lights
The Stop/Brake Lights turn ON when regenerative braking system is engaged and when the brake pedal is depressed to alert other road users that the vehicle is slowing down.
There are several Brake Lights on the vehicle including:
Center High Mounted Stop Light (CHMSL)
Brake/Stop Light
Center High Mounted Stop Light (CHMSL)
The Center High Mount Stop Light (CHMSL), located at the top of the liftgate, turns on with the brake light.
Turn Indicator Lights
Turn Lights are used to signal that the driver will change lanes or make a turn. They turn on when the driver initiates a turn using the left indicator stalk.
Reverse Lights
The Reverse Lights turn ON when the Ignition is ON and Reverse (R) is selected.
License Plate Lights
License plate lights make the license plate visible in dark conditions. When either the headlights or parking lights are turned on, the license plate lights will light up.
Rear Reflectors
The rear reflection area is used to reflect light and helps improve the visibility of vehicles at night.
CAUTION
Do not use a dry cloth or sponges to clean headlights or taillights, as they could scratch or damage the lens surface.
NOTE
In cool or damp weather, the inside of the headlights, turn signals, and Taillights may develop condensation. This will clear shortly after switching the lights ON and will not impact the service life of the lights.
Daytime Running Lights
Daytime running lights (DRL) are used to improve the visibility of vehicles on the road during the day.
WARNING
During low visibility conditions, the Daytime Running Lights system may not be able to provide adequate lighting. When needed, turn ON the headlights manually (if Auto Headlights are turned OFF) during conditions of low visibility. Failure to do so may result in a crash due to poor visibility.
The DRLs will turn OFF when:
The front position light is ON.
The front signature light is ON.
The turn signal indicators are active.
The vehicle is turned OFF.
Signature Lights
Signature Lights   are used to provide a lighting feature of the vehicle and are not intended to illuminate the road ahead.
Signature light operation:
Turn ON when the lighting mode is set to Position Lights.
Turn ON when the Headlights are ON.
Follow Me Home Lighting
When the Follow Me Home feature is enabled, the exterior lights will stay illuminated for a period of time after exiting the vehicle, before turning off.

Or, Slide down from top of touchscreen to open Notification Center.
Tap Settings   > Light Settings   > Exterior.
Choose the time delay for lights to remain ON -  Off/30s/60s/90s/120s .
To activate the Follow Me Home feature, select a duration other than  Off  and briefly flash the high beam using the left multi switch stalk before exiting the vehicle.
NOTE
Follow Me Home is triggered only when the required conditions are met. Selecting a duration does not automatically activate the lights after every drive.
The default duration is 30s.

## Wipers and Washers

Front Wipers
The Front Wipers are used to remove water, rain, snow, dirt, and debris from the vehicle’s front windshield. The wipers operate across the front windshield to give the driver better visibility.
Wiper Controls
The front wiper control stalk is located behind the steering wheel on the left hand side.
Mist: Rotate the knob   upward and hold briefly to wipe once.
Spray water: Rotate the knob   upward and hold to spray washer fluid and wipe. Release the knob to stop spraying and wiping, and the wiper will return to its initial position.
OFF: Rotate the knob   to the “ OFF ” position to turn OFF the front wiper.
Auto: Rotate the knob   to the “AUTO” position to enable automatic wiping.
LO: Rotate the knob   to the “ LO ” position for continuous low-speed wiping.
HI: Rotate the knob   to the “ HI ” position for continuous high-speed wiping.﻿
CAUTION
Before using the wipers, please clear the ice and snow on the windshield and confirm that the wiper blades are not frozen in a fixed position.
If there are wrinkles or stains on the windshield, it is necessary to clean the windshield and wiper blades. If the problem still persists, replace the wiper blades.
It is strictly prohibited to operate the wiper blades when the windshield is dry.
To prevent possible damage to the washer pump, do not operate the windshield washer when the fluid reservoir is empty.
Do not attempt to move the wipers manually, it may damage the wiper arms and other components.
Always use VinFast-approved washer fluid in the washer reservoir. Failure to follow this instruction may severely impact visibility when washer fluid is sprayed on the windshield, and might damage the windshield.
NOTE
The Wiper/Washer activation function will work only when the vehicle is in ON condition.
Check the washer fluid level regularly.
Check it often when the weather is bad.
Fill the washer fluid reservoir till full.
To prevent icing on the windshield during the colder months, turn on the defroster to heat the windshield before and during washer use.
Rear Wipers
The Rear Wiper is used to remove rain, snow, dirt, and debris from the vehicle’s rear windshield for better visibility for the driver.
Rear Wiper Controls
The rear wiper control stalk is located behind the steering wheel on the left hand side.
ON/OFF: Short press the rear left button   to toggle rear wiper continuous wiping ON/OFF.
Mist: Rotate the knob   downward and hold briefly to wipe once.
Rear Windshield Wash: Rotate the knob   downward and hold to spray washer fluid and wipe. Release the knob to stop spraying and wiping, and the wiper will return to its initial position.
To turn ON/OFF Auto Rear Wiper:
The auto rear wiper function will automatically cycle the rear wiper when the front wipers are activated and Reverse (R) gear is selected.
Tap App Library   on the touchscreen.
Or, Slide down from top of touchscreen to open Notification Center.
Tap Settings   > Vehicle Settings   > Wipers.
Use the toggle button to turn the feature On/Off.
CAUTION
Before using the Rear Wiper, clear any ice or snow from the Rear Window otherwise it may prevent Wiper Blade operating effectively. It may also cause damage to the Wiper Motor.
Do not operate the Windscreen Wipers on a dry screen. The drag on the Wiper mechanism may cause damage.
If the Wiper Blades become stuck or jammed, an electronic cut-out may temporarily stop the Wipers. If this happens, switch OFF the Wipers and the vehicle. Clear any obstructions and free the Wiper Blades, before attempting to switch ON the vehicle.
In freezing or very hot conditions ensure that the Wipers have not stuck to the Windscreen before operating. The drag on the Wiper mechanism may cause damage.
Always use VinFast approved Washer fluid in the Washer reservoir. Failure to follow this instruction may severely impact visibility when Washer fluid is sprayed on the Windshield and may damage the Windshield.
NOTE
The wipers will turn ON only when the vehicle is ON.

## Braking and Stopping

Electric Parking Brake
The Electric Parking Brake (EPB) is an electronically controlled parking brake that is designed to hold or stop the vehicle when parked. Additionally, it can also be used as an emergency brake.
When EPB is active, the   icon is displayed on the screen.
To engage the Electric Parking Brake:
Ensure the vehicle is stationary, press the P gear button on the lever to the right of the steering wheel. The vehicle will shift into Park (P) and the electronic parking brake will be activated.
WARNING
Always ensure that the electronic parking brake is activated and the vehicle is in Park (P) mode before exiting the vehicle.
NOTE
The electronic parking brake is applied to the two rear wheels and operates independently from the main braking system.
Electric parking brake will be automatically engaged when the vehicle is turned OFF, or when Auto Hold is active and the vehicle remains stationary for more than 5 minutes.
When the automatic shift-to-P function is enabled, the electronic parking brake will also be automatically activated when the driver’s door is opened.
To release the Electric Parking Brake:
Press the brake pedal and shift to Neutral (N), the electronic parking brake will be released.
Press the brake pedal and shift to D (Drive); the electronic parking brake will be released automatically. If shifting to R (Reverse), gently press the accelerator pedal to allow the system to automatically release the electronic parking brake.
CAUTION
When the vehicle is out of battery, the EPB cannot be released.
To use the Electric Parking Brake in an Emergency:
If it is not possible to park through the brake pedal, the electronic parking brake can be used for emergency braking.
Press and hold the P gear button on the lever to achieve emergency braking function.
The Electric Parking Brake will slow down the vehicle until switch is released.
CAUTION
Do not use the Electric Parking Brake to stop the vehicle, except in case of an emergency. The brake system may get damaged if the Electric Parking Brake is used repeatedly to slow down or stop the vehicle.
Auto-hold
Auto Hold ( (AVH) keeps the vehicle stationary without needing to continuously press the brake pedal, such as when stopped at a red light or in traffic jams. When Auto Hold is activated, after you press the brake pedal to bring the vehicle to a complete stop, the system will automatically hold the brakes even if you release the brake pedal.﻿ To continue moving, simply press the accelerator, and the system will automatically release the brakes.
When the vehicle is in the Auto Hold active state, to ensure safety and achieve optimal system performance, the system will automatically engage the electronic parking brake (EPB) in certain situations, such as:
Turning off the Auto Hold function via the infotainment screen without pressing the brake pedal.
When the driver’s door is opened.
Auto Hold active more than 5 minutes.
When the vehicle is stopped on a slope greater than 25%.
The system detects that the vehicle is rolling.
The driver shifts to Park (P) gear.
How to Activate/Deactivate Auto Hold:
Tap  Drive Mode    on the Driver View Area to access the Driving Preferences interface.
Scroll down to find  Auto Hold .
Use the toggle button to turn the feature On/Off.
Recognize the status on the infotainment screen:
CONDITION
SYMBOL
DESCRIPTION
Integrated IPC
Seperate IPC
AVH OFF
[OFF]: AUTO HOLD off.
AVH Ready
[White]: when Auto Hold feature is enabled via infotainment screen and Auto Hold is in READY state.
AVH Active
[Green]: The green icon indicates that the Auto Hold feature is enabled and in the active state. At this point, the driver can release the brake pedal.
Auto Hold transitions to the Active state when the vehicle is stopped, the brake pedal is firmly pressed, and the feature is activated via the screen.
AVH Failure
[Yellow]: When the Auto Hold feature malfunctions.
WARNING
Check the warning lights on the control panel. If any symbols related to brakes or Auto Hold appear, stop using the feature and inspect the vehicle.
Perform regular maintenance and ensure the sensors and braking system are checked frequently.
Always use the handbrake or electronic parking brake (EPB) and turn off the engine when leaving the vehicle.
Ensure that the Auto Hold indicator light turns green before you releasing the brake pedal.
NOTE
Auto Hold only operates in Drive (D), Reverse (R), and Neutral (N) gears (in cases where shifting from Drive or Reverse to Neutral). It does not work in the Park mode.
If the user enables both Creep Mode and Auto Hold simultaneously, Creep Mode will not activate while Auto Hold is in the active state. The user can disable Auto Hold if necessary.
When ACC function is active or resumes from Standby, the Auto Hold feature transits from Active to Fade out to avoid conflicts.
When the user stops the vehicle on a slope, presses the brake to keep the vehicle stationary, and activates the Auto Hold feature, the indicator will turn green immediately.
Anti-lock Braking System (ABS)
The Anti-Lock Braking System (ABS) prevents the wheels from locking and helps maintaining steering during hard braking events. The speed of each wheel is monitored and braking pressure on each wheel is adjusted so that the wheels continue to rotate and don’t lock and slide.
When ABS is working, you will feel brake pedal vibration and may hear noise, which is a normal phenomenon when ABS is working.
WARNING
The use of tires with insufficient adhesion (such as excessively worn tires on snow covered roads) may result in ABS not working effectively.
The ABS warning light   and Brake warning light   briefly illuminate when the vehicle is started. If the lights do not illuminate, contact VinFast Service Center to have the vehicle inspected and repaired.
If the ABS warning light is on and it remains on, you may have a problem with the ABS system. Do not drive your vehicle, contact VinFast Service Center to have the vehicle inspected.
ABS helps maintain steering control during hard braking, but it cannot overcome the effects of excessive speed, unsafe following distances, or reckless driving.
CAUTION
Maintaining strong and stable pressure on the brake pedal is crucial in emergency braking situations. Moving the foot off the brake pedal will increase the braking distance.
ABS performance is impacted by snow, ice, roads covered with dirt or gravel. Drive carefully, always keep enough room ahead to stop the vehicle.
Electronic Brake Distribution (EBD)
Electronic Brake Distribution (EBD) optimizes the braking performance of the vehicle under different operating conditions. It electronically adjusts the brake bias in between the wheels (front and rear) based on road conditions, vehicle load, speed, traction, etc.
WARNING
EBD system does not compensate or prevent accidents caused by dangerous driving or high speed maneuverer.
Brake Assist (BA)
The Brake Assist (BA) system recognizes when the driver has applied the brake suddenly and applies additional brake pressure to assist the driver in minimizing vehicle stopping distance. BA system works to assist the vehicles braking system to engage ABS as fast as possible and will disengage upon release of the brake pedal.
WARNING
Do not completely rely on ﻿Brake Assist system﻿ as it does not compensate or prevent accidents caused by dangerous driving or high speed turning maneuvers.
Electronic Stability Control (ESC)
The Electronic Stability Control System (ESC) helps to stabilize the vehicle during cornering and braking events if traction between the road surface and tires is reduced. ESC monitors individual wheel speed and driver steering angle and applies brake pressure to individual wheels to prevent vehicle understeer and oversteer, and aids the driver to keep the vehicle on it’s intended path.
The ESC light   will temporarily flash on the screen when the ESC system is actively applying brakes. In case of a malfunction, the ESC light will remain on in which case contact a VinFast Service Center to have the vehicle inspected.
To enable/disable ESC﻿:
Tap  Drive Mode    on the Driver View Area to access the Driving Preferences screen.
Scroll down to find  Electronic Stability Control (ESC) .
Change ESC setting to OFF/ON using toggle button.
WARNING
Electronic Stability Control does not compensate or prevent collisions caused by dangerous driving or turning sharply at high speeds.
CAUTION
Maintain strong and steady pressure on the brake pedal during an emergency braking situation. Do not remove your foot from the brake pedal or pump the brake pedal, as doing so can increase the braking distance.
NOTE
ESC by default will be ON every time you turn the vehicle ON.
Turning off ESC does not disable ABS or the braking system of the vehicle.
Traction Control System will also be disabled when ESC is turned OFF.
Traction Control System (TCS)
The Traction Control System (TCS) is an active safety feature which helps to limit the wheel slip and assist the driver in maintaining the control of the vehicle, especially in slippery road conditions. TCS monitors the speed of each individual wheel, and if slip is detected, it applies brake pressure to that slipping wheel in order to maximize traction. It is recommended that TCS is ON at all times for maximum safety.
The TCS light   will temporarily flash on the screen when the TCS system is actively applying brakes. In case of a malfunction is detected the TCS light   will remain ON. In this case, contact a VinFast Service Center to have your vehicle inspected.
Traction Control System is ON by default.
To enable/disable TCS﻿:
Tap  Drive Mode    on the Driver View Area to access the Driving Preferences screen.
Scroll down to find  Traction Control System (TCS) .
Change TCS setting to OFF/ON using toggle button.
WARNING
Traction Control System does not compensate or prevent collisions caused by dangerous driving or turning sharply at high speeds.
NOTE
If the vehicle is stuck while driving on snow, mud, or sand covered surfaces, or if you are using snow chains, turning TCS OFF may improve forward movement. However, it is recommended to turn ON the TCS as soon as the vehicle is mobile again.
Traction Control System will also be disabled when ESC is turned OFF.
Hill-start Assist Control (HSA)
Hill-start Assist Control (HSA) temporarily prevents the vehicle from unintended roll back when positioned on a hill or incline.﻿ The system remains activated for a period of ﻿﻿approximately 2-seconds after the driver releases the brake pedal. HSA works by temporarily holding brake pressure, after the brake pedal is released by the driver.
WARNING
Do not rely on HSA completely. It does not replace human input and intuition in any way or form. Always be aware of the surroundings and drive safely.
A heavily loaded vehicle may cause the vehicle to roll backwards slightly.
CAUTION
HSA remains active only for a period of approximately 2-seconds after releasing the brake pedal. Always be careful of the surroundings and be ready to press the brake pedal.
Roll Over Mitigation (ROM)
Roll Over Mitigation is a safety feature which anticipates the potential for wheel lift by monitoring the driver’s steering wheel angle, vehicle speed and acceleration. Roll Over Mitigation analyses the rate of change of steering wheel angle and the vehicle speed to determine the possibility of a vehicle roll. The system reduces the possibility by applying appropriate brake pressure to individual wheels.
Roll Over Mitigation cannot prevent wheel lift caused by factors such as:
Road conditions
Rough terrain
Collision with other vehicles or objects
WARNING
Factors, such as road conditions, driving conditions, and vehicle loading may influence the chances of wheel lift or rollover.
Roll Over Mitigation cannot prevent wheel lift or rollover in every situation.
Roll Over Mitigation is a safety feature .  It should not be exploited by driving the vehicle recklessly and causing danger to driver, passengers, and others.
NOTE
The curtain airbag remains or will be inflated in the event of a side impact or a rollover accident.
When inflated, the curtain airbag is designed to cover the side windows, reducing the possibility of a passenger being ejected from the vehicle in the event of an accident.
Hill Descent Control (HDC)
The Hill Descent Control (HDC) system automatically applies the brakes when the vehicle is descending a steep slope to help the driver maintain a steady and safe speed.
To enable/disable HDC﻿:
Tap  Drive Mode    on the Driver View Area to access the Driving Preferences screen.
Scroll down to find  Hill Descent Control .
Change HDC setting to OFF/ON using toggle button.
WARNING
HDC is an assistance function only. The driver remains responsible for the safe operation of the vehicle and must always pay attention to surrounding traffic conditions.
If the slope is too steep, HDC may not maintain a steady speed. Press the brake pedal to control the vehicle speed if necessary.
CAUTION
HDC can be activated when the vehicle speed is between 8 km/h and 35 km/h. HDC will automatically deactivate when the vehicle speed exceeds 60 km/h.
If the brake system temperature becomes too high, HDC may be unavailable or automatically deactivated.
Emergency Signal System (ESS)
The Emergency Signal System (ESS) temporarily activates the hazard lights when the vehicle detects an emergency braking situation. Rapid flashing of the hazard lights helps warn nearby vehicles of a rapidly slowing vehicle and alerts them to maintain a safe distance to avoid collision.
The hazard lights stops flashing once the emergency braking event ends. If the hazard lights continue to flash, contact the nearest VinFast Service Center.
For information on Hazard Warning Flashers, see >   Exterior Lighting > Hazard Warning Flashers  .
Regenerative Braking
The vehicle is equipped with a Regenerative Braking System. The system converts kinetic energy usually released as heat during a braking event into electrical energy. The recovered energy can then be used to replenish the HV battery, leading to increased vehicle range.
The system gets activated as soon as the accelerator pedal is released. It will gradually slow the vehicle down and start recharging the HV battery.
The energy recovery can be monitored on the Power and Regen Gauge, which is displayed in the status bar above the Drive Mode icon on the infotainment screen.
NOTE
The level of regenerative braking may be reduced or unavailable when the battery is fully charged or when the battery temperature is too low or too high.
To turn ON/OFF Power & Regen Display:
The Power & Regen Display shows power consumption and energy regeneration information while driving. It is only available while the vehicle is in motion.
Tap  Drive Mode    on the Driver View Area to access the Driving Preferences interface.
Scroll down to find  Power & Regen Display .
Use the toggle button to turn the feature On/Off.
Adjust amount of Regenerative Braking
The vehicle provides levels of regenerative braking ( Off, Low, Mid and High) . The levels can be switched using the toggle button under Regenerative Braking.
Tap  Drive Mode    on the Driver View Area to access the Driving Preferences interface.
Find  Regenerative Braking .
Tap to turn off the regenerative braking or switch regenerative braking level.
WARNING
Do not consider regenerative braking as the primary braking of the vehicle; it is only a supplementary feature.

## Economy & Environment

Economical Driving
Economical driving can help to improve energy efficiency, road safety, and prevent abnormal wear of vehicle.
Refer to the following recommendations for economical driving:
Avoid harsh braking: The Regenerative Braking System is a key enabler of economic driving. The system converts some of the vehicle’s kinetic energy into electrical energy which is then used to recharge the vehicle’s batteries and extend the driving range of the vehicle. Taking advantage of Regenerative Braking by reducing friction on the brakes will significantly improve the driver’s ability to drive economically and to avoid harsh acceleration and braking. See >   Braking and Stopping > Regenerative Braking  .
Maintain an efficient speed: High speed driving increases energy consumption, discharging the battery faster, and therefore reducing the vehicle’s driving range.
Reconsider use of heating and air conditioning: The vehicle’s Heating, Lighting, Climate Control, Audio, and Infotainment systems consume energy from the vehicle’s battery system and will therefore reduce the vehicle’s driving range. Whenever it is safe and convenient to do so reduce the usage of these systems to drive economically.
Tire pressure: Always keep the tire inflation pressure at the recommended level to reduce unnecessary rolling resistance that decreases efficiency of the battery.
Battery management: To improve the health and life of the battery keep the vehicle in a well-maintained condition. Better battery health will improve the driving range. Minimize the use of DC Fast Chargers, particularly Super-Fast Chargers, to improve the Battery life. Do not allow the battery charge to drop to a very low level.
Remove unnecessary weight: Energy consumption can be reduced by removing excessive or unnecessary weight, when not in use and it is convenient to do so, improve vehicle driving range by removing items such as unused child seats, disposable, personal items, etc.
Maintain distance: Maintain an adequate distance from other vehicles to avoid emergency braking.
Wheel alignment: Have the wheel alignment checked as recommended in the maintenance schedule. Incorrect wheel alignment can lead to increased tire wear and power consumption.
Windows: It is always recommended to drive with all the windows closed if possible.
Driving Statistics (if available)
Driving Statistics displays the history of the battery consumption, distance, and power use of the vehicle over a chosen period. This data is also available to view via the VinFast App.
NOTE
This menu is only shown when fitted (for the markets that has internet connection).
To open driving statistics:
Tap  Profile Name  on the status bar.
Tap  My Profile > Profile Settings > Driving Statistics   .
Driving Statistics Graph    will be displayed on the screen.

Statistics will be shown for the selected Time Duration.

## Cold Weather

Cold Weather
Overview
Winter months and lower temperatures may cause some changes in the operation of the vehicle. It is important to be aware of the effects of cold weather so that you can prepare accordingly. When snow and ice accumulate on the vehicle, the battery, tires, wiper fluid, and wipers can freeze. To achieve maximum range and performance, it is helpful to warm the cabin and battery before driving.
When battery is in cold condition, a snowflake icon appears beside battery capacity bar. Battery capacity bar also changes to amber color.
Impact of cold weather
Battery at risk:
Temperatures lower than -30°C ( -22 °F ) can cause issues to the battery in terms of performance and range. It is recommended to select ECO drive mode to help extend the range when driving in cold weather.
Frozen Door Handles, Windows, Mirrors:
In winter conditions, snow or ice may buildup within the door handles, windows and vehicle mirrors. It prevents the vehicle door, window and mirror from operating properly.
Doors:
Apply some extra force while pulling on the door handles to break it free of ice.
Open and close the door handle a few more times to release any remaining ice buildup.
Ensure the door handle is fully retracted before entering the vehicle, and ensure that the door is fully closed before you drive off.
Windows:
See >   Climate Control System > Front Windscreen Defrost and Defog   and see >   Climate Control System > Rear Demist  .
Alternatively, the VinFast App can be used to defrost the windows before entering the vehicle.
Mirrors:
The ice can hinder the folding or unfolding of exterior side mirrors. Therefore, it is recommended to turn OFF Mirror Auto Fold so that they do not fold when the vehicle is locked. See >   Mirrors > Side Mirrors  .
Tire inflation issues:
Fluctuating temperatures have impacts on the level of inflation in tires, which makes it extremely important to check the tire pressures frequently in cold conditions. Use winter tires to increase traction in snowy or icy conditions. See >   Wheels and Tires > Tire Pressure  .
Frozen windshield wipers:
Cold temperatures can also cause your windshield wipers to freeze, which can be dangerous. If snow or ice is expected when parked, consider tilting the windshield wipers up.
Lighting operation:
Remove any snow, frost or ice that may cover the indicators, head lights and brake lights, before driving to ensure safe vehicle operation.
While Driving
Cold conditions increase energy consumption due to more power which is needed for driving, cabin heating, and battery charging. Energy consumption can be reduced by following these tips:
Maintain slow and steady speed while driving and avoid rapid braking and accelerations.
Regenerative braking
Depending on the battery temperature, regenerative braking may be limited. The battery warms up as you drive and regenerative power increases. See >   Braking and Stopping > Regenerative Braking  .
Cooling system
Before use cooling system be ensure the bumper cleaned snow and ice, it can be appeared fault in the winter time because the snow and ice interferes good working of moveable radiator grill.
Brake Performance in Cold Weather Conditions:
When the vehicle is driven or parked in snowy, icy, or extremely cold conditions, ice or frost may form on brake components. Under these conditions, braking effectiveness may be temporarily reduced during the initial brake applications.
Before driving, inspect the brake system for any visible ice or snow accumulation, ensure the brake pedal moves smoothly, and verify brake effectiveness in a safe area. If braking performance is reduced, apply the brakes gently several times. Brake performance will gradually return to normal as the brake friction surfaces warm up and any ice, frost, or moisture is removed.
Drive carefully and maintain a greater distance from the vehicle ahead when operating the vehicle under severe winter conditions.
During extended driving periods with little or no brake application, periodically apply the brakes lightly to confirm proper brake operation and help keep the brake friction surfaces dry and free from ice accumulation.
WARNING
Always ensure that the electronic parking brake is activated and the vehicle is in Park (P) mode before exiting the vehicle.
Do not use the cruise control on slippery roads such as snowy or icy roads. This may cause loss of vehicle control.
Do not use a combination of different tires in extreme weather conditions.

## Flood Precautions

Flood Precautions
CAUTION
It is not recommended to drive in flooded roads or areas.
Water may enter critical vehicle systems, especially the electrical and high-voltage (HV) systems, which may result in the following hazards:
Electric shock
Fire or overheating
Damage to the battery, electric motor, and control modules
Loss of vehicle operability
VinFast does not recommend driving the vehicle through flooded areas. Only proceed through flooded roads when no safer alternative is available.
Before Driving Through Flooded Areas
Check and assess the water depth before proceeding.
Do not drive if the water level exceeds the vehicle ground clearance
Observe road surface conditions and water flow.
When Driving Through Flooded Areas
Operating Guidelines:
Drive at a low speed (below 10 km/h).
Maintain a steady speed and avoid sudden acceleration or deceleration.
Drive straight and avoid abrupt steering inputs.
Maintain a safe distance from other vehicles.
Turn off the air conditioning system to reduce the risk of water being drawn into the vehicle through the blower system and to minimize electrical load.
Use an appropriate drive mode. Switch to ECO or NORMAL mode (if equipped) to improve accelerator control and reduce wheel slip.
Actions to Avoid
Do not stop the vehicle in flooded areas.
Do not accelerate suddenly or create large waves in front of the vehicle.
Avoid reversing in water unless necessary to exit the flooded area safely.
Do not open the vehicle doors while in flooded conditions.
Large water waves may allow water to enter electrical components and cause vehicle damage.
WARNING
After Driving Through Flooded Areas
-Apply the brakes several times to help dry the braking system.
-Check brake responsiveness while driving at low speed.
-Monitor the vehicle for any warning messages or indicators.
Precautions for Continued Operation
-Water, mud, or silt may reduce braking performance.
-Braking distance may increase.
-Maintain a safe following distance and avoid sudden braking.
Risk of Flood-Related Damage
Driving through deep water may cause severe damage to:
-High-voltage (HV) battery
-Electric motor
-Drivetrain system
-Steering system
-Electrical system and control modules
Water, mud, leaves, or debris accumulated after driving through flooded areas may affect:
-Corrosion
-Electrical short circuits
-The A/C condenser, and the cooling performance of the system
-Intermittent or delayed system malfunctions
Flood Damage Is Not Covered Under Warranty
Damage affecting the high-voltage (HV) system.
If the vehicle is submerged in water above its ground clearance.
-Water may enter the high-voltage system.
-This may result in loss of insulation and electrical leakage.
-Potential hazards include electric shock, fire, or severe vehicle damage.
Damage related to the high-voltage system may not occur immediately and can appear after a period of time.
Situations Requiring Immediate Action
Stop the vehicle and contact VinFast immediately if any of the following conditions occur:
The vehicle cannot enter READY mode.
Warning messages related to the electrical system or battery are displayed.
The vehicle operates abnormally or experiences power loss.
A burning smell, smoke, or unusual noises are detected.
Required Actions
Stop the vehicle in a safe location.
Power off the vehicle.
Do not continue operating the vehicle.
Do not charge the vehicle under any circumstances.
Exit the vehicle if there are signs of unsafe conditions.
Contact VinFast support for assistance.
Handling a Flood-Damaged Vehicle
If the vehicle has been submerged or exposed to flooding:
Do Not
Restart the vehicle.
Charge the vehicle.
Attempt to inspect, disassemble, or repair any components.
Mandatory Requirements
Contact VinFast for assistance.
Transport the vehicle to the nearest authorized dealer or service center.
Repairs must only be performed by authorized VinFast service facilities.
Repairs performed by unauthorized facilities may not ensure vehicle safety and may affect warranty coverage.
Recommendations
Avoid driving through flooded areas whenever possible.
Do not take risks if the water depth cannot be determined.
Choose a safer alternative route whenever available.

## Infotainment Screen

Infotainment Screen Overview
You can control or adjust vehicle features and functions through the infotainment screen. Depending on the vehicle variant, the infotainment screen has also replaced the traditional combination instrument panel to display important information such as vehicle speed, driving status, warnings, indications, and more.
Images and features in this chapter are for illustration purposes only and may vary depending on vehicle variant and selected software.
Infotainment Screen Layout
Driver view area
Displays vehicle status and driving related information, including vehicle speed, warning icons, and battery status. Provides shortcuts for various vehicle functions. The displayed content and available features may vary depending on the vehicle version.
Status bar
Displays important information such as the VinFast logo, phone connection status, profile avatar and status icons.
Home page/App view
Opened app and widgets.
Bottom bar
Provides access to the App Library and to various functions, such as lighting controls, climate controls, and returning to the home screen.
App Library
The App Library displays all the Apps available on the infotainment system.
To enter App Library:
Tap App Library   on the Infotainment screen.
All available Apps will be displayed.
Infotainment Screen Use
Use the infotainment screen to control the vehicle and infotainment settings.
It is recommended that users familiarize themselves with all Infotainment screen related content presented in the Owner’s Manual. This will enable the driver to focus their attention on driving, while experiencing the best performance and features offered by the system.
WARNING Do not use the Infotainment screen frequently and avoid reading any texts while driving. Extended focus on the Infotainment screen may lead to an accident, and can cause serious injuries or death.
CAUTION
Use high quality microfiber cloth to clean the Infotainment screen display. Do not use any abrasive material or cleaning solutions on the infotainment screen display as it can cause permanent damage.

Browsing Lists:
To scroll down the browsing lists, drag from bottom to top.
To scroll up the browsing lists, drag from top to bottom.
Panning:

View another location on a map or a zoomed in image without changing its magnification by using the panning function.

To Edit and Remove Key Fob Linking
Tap  Profile settings  >  Authentication .
To activate/deactivate Key Fob Linking, tap  Activate / Deactivate .
To unlink key fob, tap on  Unlink .
Remote Control (if available)
To Link VinFast App:
Tap  Pair now .
To link from VinFast app, scan  QR code .
After confirmation, a notification will pop up on screen.
To Unlink VinFast App:
Tap  Unpair device .
Tap  Unpair  to confirm.
Personalization
This allows the driver to save/set Steering Wheel, Side Mirrors and Driver Seat positions to a profile. These settings will be available upon the next profile login.
To save/delete positions:
Tap  Profile settings  >  Personalization .
To save the current position of Steering Wheel / Side Mirrors / Driver Seat, tap  Save New .
To delete a saved position, tap  Delete .
Valet Mode (if available)
This feature, when activated, disables certain system features and functions preventing unintended use by another person or access to personal information.
Valet mode uses limited features. When active, the following applies:
Store setting Valet mode in NvM.
Delay 5 seconds then stop Wifi connection (if any)
Stop Bluetooth connection (if any)
Disconnect USB connection (if any)
Turn Rear Seat Entertainment off (when fitted)
Wi-Fi and Bluetooth management are not accessible
All entertainment functions are disabled (media, phone, game, maps, 3rd apps...) & stop Media/Radio (if playing)
All vehicle settings are not accessible
User Profiles are not accessible
SmartLinks (Android Auto, Apple CarPlay) are disabled
Voice Assistant is disable
Embedded Maps are not shown
Auto updating User Profile data is disabled
FOTA/SOTA is disabled (except show new Campaign information)
Repair history is not shown
Some ADAS functions shall be deactivated (ADAS functions including: ACC/HWA/FAPA, RP (If has) are disabled)
Display a notification to inform that vehicle has been limit speed
Activation
Valet mode can be activated by any of the following methods:
Changing profile to Valet.
VinFast App (if available).
To activate Valet Mode by changing Profile
On the status bar, tap the Profile Name.
To activate Valet Mode, tap on  Valet .
NOTE
Guest profiles cannot activate Valet Mode.
To activate Valet Mode by VinFast App (if available)
Ensure that the VinFast App is successfully paired with the vehicle.
From the home screen, tap on Remote Control.
On Valet Mode feature, select On/Off.
Valet Mode Settings Overview
Following settings can be adjusted:
Valet Mode PIN  - Edit Valet PIN.
Speed Limit  - Limit the maximum speed of the vehicle.
NOTE
The speed limit can be adjusted in the range of 40-110 km/h.
To access Valet Mode settings:
Tap  App Library    on the touchscreen.
Or, Slide down from top of touchscreen to open Notification Center.
Tap  Settings    >  Security Settings    >  Valet Mode .
Deactivation
Valet Mode can be deactivated by using Valet Mode PIN.
To deactivate by using Valet Mode PIN:
Tap  Exit Valet Mode  in Profile Dropdown menu.
Or, Tap  Exit Valet Mode  in App View.
Enter a valid PIN.
NOTE
Valet Mode cannot be exited while driving or by restarting the vehicle.
Camp Mode
Camp Mode is a convenient feature that enhances user comfort when using the vehicle for resting or outdoor camping.
Activation
Preconditions for Camp Mode activation:
The vehicle must be in Park (P).
The vehicle must be ON.
Battery state of charge must be greater than 25%.
NOTE
Camp Mode can be activated when the battery level is below 25% if the vehicle is charging.
To activate Camp Mode:
Slide down from the top status bar of Infotainment screen.
From the vehicle function shortcut selection list, click on Camp Mode  .
NOTE
Camp mode can also be activated via the vehicle shutdown notification.
Deactivation:
Camp Mode can be deactivated in any of the following conditions:
Tap  Exit Camp Mode  on the Infotainment screen.
Change gear mode to Drive (D)/Reverse (R)/Neutral (N).
Turn on the Pet/Valet/Wash Mode.
Restarting MHU.
High voltage system has issue.
NOTE
Camp mode cannot be accessed if the battery percentage is below 25%.
Camp Mode will be automatically deactivated if battery percentage is below 15%.
Additionally, users can also enable or disable Camp Mode through the VinFast app (if available).
Wash Mode (if available)
Wash Mode prepares the vehicle for an automatic car wash by closing all the﻿ windows, locking all the doors, locking the charge port door, and disabling the windshield wipers, etc.
Preconditions for Wash Mode activation:
The vehicle must be in Park (P)
Hood must be closed
Charging port must be closed
Liftgate must be closed
All doors must be closed
To activate Wash Mode:
Slide down from the top status bar of Infotainment screen.
From the vehicle function shortcut selection list, click on Wash Mode  .
Make sure all the pre-conditions are met to start Wash Mode.
If any of the pre-conditions are not met the  X  icon will be displayed on the infotainment screen.
Tap  Start Wash Mode .
The vehicle systems will check a list of additional conditions which are required to be compliant for Wash Mode activation.
WARNING
Apply brake carefully after washing. Washing may reduce the vehicle’s braking system effectiveness. Braking system will be restored after a few minutes of driving.
CAUTION
Ensure the vehicle remains in Wash Mode until the vehicle car wash process is complete.
Automatic car wash machines can damage the vehicle. Such damages are not covered under Manufacturer Warranty.
NOTE
Ensure that the car wash is suitable as per the vehicle dimensions.
In Wash Mode, the side mirrors can be folded/unfolded manually using the switch on the screen.
Deactivation:
Tap  Exit Wash Mode  on the Infotainment screen.
NOTE
Wash Mode will be automatically deactivated if the driver engages Drive (D) and vehicle speed is greater than 16km/h (10 mph).
Pet Mode (if available)
This feature allows the pets to be left unattended in the vehicle by creating and maintaining a comfortable environment.
WARNING
Pet Mode is not intended for people.
Always stay in close proximity to the vehicle when using Pet Mode.
Check the countries laws and regulations for any restrictions on leaving pets unattended in vehicles.
Do not completely rely on Pet mode. Never leave pets in the vehicle unattended for long periods of time.
Constantly monitor the vehicle temperature from mobile device.
Always monitor battery charge levels from your mobile device, since a low battery state of charge will deactivate Pet Mode.
Activation
Preconditions for activating Pet Mode:
Pet mode is activated only if the vehicle’s battery state of charge is greater than 25%.
Vehicle Gear position must be in Park (P) and the EPB must be engaged.
The vehicle is powered on or is in charging status.
To activate Pet Mode:
Slide down from the top status bar of Infotainment screen.
From the vehicle function shortcut selection list, click on Pet Mode  .
To edit Pet Mode message:
Tap  Edit message .
Enter the content in the text field.
Tap  Save message .
Deactivation
Pet Mode can be deactivated in any of the following conditions:
Tap  Exit Pet Mode  on the Infotainment screen.
Change gear mode to Drive (D)/Reverse (R)/Neutral (N).
Turn on the Camp/Valet/Wash Mode.
The driver door is opened.
Restarting MHU.
High voltage system has issue.
NOTE
Pet Mode will be automatically deactivated if battery percentage is below 15%.
Additionally, users can also enable or disable Pet Mode through the VinFast app (if available).

## Multimedia & Utilities

Overview and Key Features
The infotainment system can be controlled by using the infotainment screen. It is used for playing various types of media sources.
Radio - Use Radio to listen to FM radio frequency band. See >  Multimedia & Utilities  > Radio  .
Bluetooth Audio - When connected to a valid Bluetooth device you can listen to stored or streamed audio via Bluetooth Media. See >  Multimedia & Utilities  > Bluetooth Audio  .
USB Media - When connected to a valid USB device you can listen to available audio and video via USB Media. See >  Multimedia & Utilities  > USB Media  .
Browser - Use Browser to browse the Internet from the vehicle’s infotainment screen display. See >  Multimedia & Utilities  > Browser  .
WARNING
Do not use the Infotainment screen when driving, as distraction from the road can cause an accident leading to serious injury and death.

Station List
The radio station list can be updated by scanning for available nearby stations in each radio frequency band. If available, the station name and radio frequency is displayed on the screen. The  Radio Bar    below the list of stations allows the seek/scan and station skip functions.
To manually search a station:
Tap Search   icon   on top right corner.
Enter station frequency in Search field.
Matching stations will appear below the search field. Select your preferred station.
NOTE

Radio Favorites
Favorites can be saved from FM﻿ stations lists.
To save a Favorites station:
From FM﻿ station list, tap the heart icon next to the station you wish to add as a Favorites﻿.
The  Heart  icon will illuminate.﻿
The station will now appear in Favorites list﻿.
To view Favorites:
To view the list of Favorites Stations, tap  Favorites  in the Radio App.
To remove a Favorites station:
From Favorites list, tap  Three Dots  icon next to a Favorites and select  Remove Favorite .
OR
To remove from Favorites, from the main station list tap the  Heart  icon.
To rename a favorite station:
Select the favorite station to be renamed and tap  Three Dots  icon.
Tap  Rename .
Change name.
Tap  Save .
To top your favorite radio station:
Select the radio station to be topped and tap  Three Dot  icon.
Tap  Move to top .
The radio station has been topped up.
To rearrange favorite Stations:
Tap and hold the station to rearrange in the station list.
Drag the station to desired position.
Release station. The Favorites station will drop and snap into highlighted position.
WARNING
Continuous use of Infotainment screen controls when driving may lead to distraction causing an accident, leading to serious injuries or death.
Sustained exposure to high sound levels greater than 85 decibels may cause hearing damage.
NOTE
The Favorites list will be sorted in sequential order with the most recently added station at the top.
Some radio stations may be out of range. These stations will remain stored under Favorites but will be automatically removed from the FM list.
A maximum of 30 FM stations can be favorited.

## Drive view

Overview Driver View Area
Vehicles with Integrated IPC
The Driver View Area is located on the left side of the Infotainment screen and displays vehicle and system status information along with providing access or adjustments to key vehicle controls. The Driver View Area replaces many of the displays and controls traditionally supplied through an instrument cluster or physical switches and buttons.
Key features of Driver View Area are listed in Display Overview:
Drive mode
Displays the selected drive mode
READY indicator light
The indicator light comes on, indicating that the vehicle is ready for driving
Gear selected
Displays current selected gear
Warning lights and indicators
Displays all critical vehicle alert Telltales and Status lights
Regen view
Displays the working status of regenerative braking
Speedometer
Displays current speed of the vehicle
Battery information
Displays the status of the battery (remaining battery, available for travel)
ODO/Trip Information
Displays the odometer reading of vehicle
Car control area
Provides quick access to control and adjust certain vehicle features
Seatbelt reminder
Displays the seatbelt warning Telltale and Status lights
Vehicles with Separate IPC
The Driver View Area on the instrument cluster displays critical driving-related information, including vehicle status, warnings, and driving data.
Key features of Driver View Area are listed in Display Overview:
Drive mode
Displays the selected drive mode
READY indicator light
The indicator light comes on, indicating that the vehicle is ready for driving
Gear selected
Displays current selected gear
Warning lights and indicators
Displays all critical vehicle alert Telltales and Status lights
Power & Regen view
Displays the energy consumption rate and the charge/discharge status
Speedometer
Displays current speed of the vehicle
Battery information
Displays the status of the battery (remaining battery, available for travel)
ODO/Trip Information
Displays the odometer reading of vehicle
Driving Information
Displays vehicle status, charging state, ADAS information, and some critical information
Entertainment area
Displays corresponding features when functions are running, such as ADAS prompt, active call, multimedia, turn-by-turn navigation, tire status indicator (TPMS), and weather
Seatbelt reminder
Displays the seatbelt warning Telltale and Status lights
Warning Lights and Indicators
All important vehicle system related warnings and alerts are shown on the Infotainment screen display in the lower section of Driver View Area. Tap on the pop-up to view further detail of each warning.
In addition to a warning notification, a corresponding telltale may also illuminate on the screen. Telltales will stay illuminated until the vehicle fault is repaired or resolved.
Telltale Icons:
Position Lights
Low Beam
Turn Indicator/Hazard Lights
Vehicle Ready to Drive
High Beam
ABS Malfunction
Park Brake Malfunction
ESC/TCS OFF
Low Power Mode
Safety Warnings
Parking Brake Applied
Brake System Faults
EV System Malfunction
Airbag Malfunction
Seatbelt Reminder
Auto Hold Active
Door not closed reminder
Tailgate not closed reminder

Tap this button to open/close the trunk .

Tap this button to open the Mirror Adjustment interface and adjust mirror-related functions as needed.

Tap this button to turn on/off the rear window lock.
Drive button
Tap this button to open the Driving Preferences interface and quickly control driving-related functions.
